Round 1, car `tooling-50-32-car-r1`, commits `f858aa1` / `b48639d` / `2555266`, base `5ea6c0c`.
Worktree `C:\Users\Chris\git\starcar-wt\review-tooling-50-32`, HEAD verified `2555266e790a552ca5ad230342a0dde61ef77c02` before any work.

The REJECT rests on things I **observed**, not read: a guard that stayed green under the exact failure signature it claims to catch, a measured silent-loss race, a measured day-one false-positive load, and two live design/setup documents left asserting things the same commit made false.

## MAJOR FINDINGS

### M1 — The narrowed settings-shape guard does not catch the documented failure signature. **Watched; it did not fire.**
`scripts/tests/SessionStartWiring.Tests.ps1:51-55` (narrowed to `hooks[4]` only) + `scripts/tests/SessionStartReport.Tests.ps1:169-183` (the replacement invariant).

Adjudication **A1: coverage narrowed in SUBSTANCE, and I proved it.** The original assertion at `f858aa1` was a **whitelist** (`^sh \.claude/hooks/[A-Za-z0-9_-]+\.sh$` over all five lines). The replacement for lines 0–3 is a **blacklist** (must contain the pipe; must not contain `sh -c` or `'`). Whitelist → blacklist is a real loss of coverage, and the hole contains the actual failure class.

**Fault injection D** (reverted): I replaced `.claude/settings.json:55` with

```
if true; then sh .claude/hooks/session-start-checkpoint-reconcile.sh; printf "%s\n" "{\"x\":1}"; fi | sh .claude/hooks/session-start-record.sh
```

— an inline `if…fi` compound plus escaped-JSON `printf`, i.e. the exact shape the design records as breaking Copilot (`docs/design/2026-07-24-family-agnostic-harness-design.md:65`: ``syntax error: unexpected end of file from `if' command``). It contains no `sh -c` and no single quote. **Observed: SessionStartWiring 5/5 passed, SessionStartReport 10/10 passed — 15/15 GREEN.** The original assertion rejects that string (`-match '^sh \.claude/hooks/…$'` → `False`, verified).

The disclosed rationale is therefore **wrong, not merely narrowed**: `SessionStartWiring.Tests.ps1:26-28` claims *"The broader 'no fancy quoting anywhere in SessionStart' invariant (the actual failure signature: nested `sh -c` / single quotes) now lives in SessionStartReport.Tests.ps1."* The actual documented signature is an inline `if` compound, and the replacement is blind to it. Disclosed-but-wrong does not clear review.

*Remedy is cheap:* keep a whitelist that permits exactly the new legitimate shape, e.g. `^sh \.claude/hooks/[A-Za-z0-9_-]+\.sh( \| sh \.claude/hooks/session-start-record\.sh( --reset)?)?$`, applied to all five lines.

### M2 — Dead citation landed in the same commit that made it false; `docs/setup.md` left stale.
`docs/design/2026-07-24-family-agnostic-harness-design.md:67` (added by `b48639d`) ends: *"Until then, `docs/setup.md`'s restart-gated paragraph carries the same disclosure."*

I opened it. `docs/setup.md` at HEAD contains **zero** matches for `session-start-report`, `session-start-record`, or the delivery mechanism (grep for `restart-gated|session-start-report|session-start-record|fifth SessionStart` returns only lines 90, 97, 98, 110 — all about Task 1's fifth line). `b48639d` does not touch `docs/setup.md` at all.

So the live APPROVED design asserts a sibling document carries a disclosure it does not carry, and the user-facing setup doc — the one a stranger reads to learn what is restart-gated — gained a new restart-gated item silently. Both halves of the repo's own rule are broken: *"the commit that invalidates a document updates that document, in the same commit"* and *"open every file:line a document cites and confirm it says what the citation claims."*

### M3 — The diff moves the four guard lines out of the intersection dialect that D5 and §5 still prescribe, and neither was amended.
`docs/design/2026-07-24-family-agnostic-harness-design.md:94` (D5, a surviving APPROVED decision): *"Carried verbatim into D4's scope: **intersection dialect for the four SessionStart guards** (one manifest)…"*
Same document `:117` (§5 mechanism item 6): *"`.claude/settings.json` (D5): **four SessionStart lines to intersection dialect**; `subagent-stop-probe.sh` key fix."*

By the car's own definition of that dialect (`SessionStartWiring.Tests.ps1:46`, `^sh \.claude/hooks/NAME\.sh$`), `.claude/settings.json:51,55,59,63` are **no longer in it** after `b48639d`. The car amended §2c (line 67) but left D5 and §5.6 stating the superseded prescription. A future car implementing §5 would read line 117 as binding and "fix" the pipes away.

Compounding, and unnamed anywhere in the landed prose: the four guard lines were the **only** SessionStart lines observed to work under Copilot (`:65`), and this commit adds a shell metacharacter to all four on the strength of an argument (`SessionStartReport.Tests.ps1:172-176`: *"a plain two-stage pipe carries neither `-c` nor any quote character"*) rather than an observation. If Copilot's compat layer does not evaluate pipes, this commit **regresses** the four guards from working-but-silent to not-executing, on the runtime it exists to serve. The restart-gated trigger at `:67` would catch it, which is why this is filed as documentation staleness plus an unstated risk rather than a proven defect — but the risk belongs in the design, not only in a test comment.

### M4 — The truncation race is not closed; silent loss **measured at 2/15** under parallel hook execution.
`.claude/hooks/session-start-record.sh:39-41` (reset) + `.claude/settings.json:51` (`--reset` on the first line only).

The brief ordered this handled. The car's mechanism handles the *reset-per-guard* variant and depends on an **unstated, unverified assumption that the five SessionStart hook commands execute sequentially in array order**. Claude Code documents that all matching hooks for an event run in **parallel**; nothing in `settings.json`, the script, the tests, or the design states or probes this assumption, and JSON array order is not an execution-order contract.

Measured, in this worktree:
- **Real four guards, launched concurrently, 12 trials:** marker present, 6 `[checkpoint]` lines, 5 `[retro]` lines, **0/12 loss** — the real guards are slow enough (~34 ms each) that line 1's truncate always won.
- **Adversarial skew** (`--reset` line racing one instant-emitting guard), 15 trials: **the fast guard's output was LOST in 2/15 trials (13%)**. The `--reset` truncate landed *after* the other guard had already appended.

Failure mode is silent loss of a guard's entire output with no signal (Law 4). The car's own script header (`:20-25`) states the race as solved. It is solved only for the variant that was tested; the tests exercise strictly sequential calls (`SessionStartReport.Tests.ps1:98-124`), never concurrency.

*The class of remedy:* make freshness a property of the file rather than of an ordering — e.g. every invocation truncates iff the existing marker is older than N seconds / carries a different session key, so no line needs to be "first."

### M5 — The reconciler was wired into the nightly goodnight ritual without ever being run against the real corpus; on day one it exits 1 with 9 gaps, **100% of them expected pre-producer firings**.
`.claude/skills/goodnight/SKILL.md:78-85` wires it into the standing 5b sweep. Nothing in `2555266`'s commit message, the script header, or the SKILL.md text states what it says on the real data.

I ran it against the real probe log and real store (read-only):

```
EXIT=1  --  9 GAP lines, 7 distinct agent_ids
[reconcile] GAP - agent_id=adba6552daddbe6db logged_at= missing_kind=returned ...
[reconcile] GAP - agent_id=a88e7dadda60940ac logged_at=2026-07-22T14:22:40.433421+00:00 ...
   (…through 2026-07-22T16:35:08.820923+00:00)
```

Corpus: 1320 probe lines, 68 with non-empty `agent_type`, 46 distinct agents. **Every one of the 9 gaps is at or before `2026-07-22T16:35:08Z`; the earliest producer-written record in the store is `2026-07-22T16:40:01Z`** (57 returned records carry runtime-id subjects; the 7 gap ids have zero records of any kind). They are pre-producer-epoch firings — structurally expected, not instances of the `#32` class the script exists to detect.

This is precisely the scar this repo already paid: *"expected/placeholder patterns are NOTES, defects are FLAGS — an instrument that cries wolf is worse than no instrument."* A nightly ritual step that reports the same 9 non-defects forever teaches the conductor to ignore it, which destroys the one signal `#32` was filed to create. The verification-honesty rule also binds: a claim about a detector needs the real-corpus observation, and none was made.

*Cheap remedies:* a producer-epoch floor (`-Since`, defaulting to the store's earliest producer record), or a one-time `presumed-lost` closure for the 7 — but whichever is chosen, the day-one output must be measured and disclosed.

## MINOR FINDINGS

- **m1 — `.gitignore:2`** adds `.claude/session-start-report.txt` with no `#50` citation. The file demonstrably *can* carry comments — see the `#33` block at `.gitignore:6-8` — so the "files that cannot carry a comment" carve-out does not apply. (`.claude/probe-logs/`, `.ci-checks/` predate the standard; no backfill, correctly.)
- **m2 — `.claude/hooks/session-start-record.sh:34`** resolves `REPORT_FILE` from a bare relative path. The house pattern for hooks is `${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-.}` (`starcar-producer-launch.sh:10`, `starcar-producer-stop.sh:11`), which is strictly more robust and degrades to the same thing. Works today under Claude Code (the probe log's own `cwd` field shows `C:\Users\Chris\git\starcar`); Copilot's hook cwd is unobserved — and Copilot is the target.
- **m3 — Law 1, unknown renders as unknown.** Against the real log's first line (which predates `_probe_logged_at`) the reconciler prints `logged_at=` blank rather than naming it unknown. Observed.
- **m4 — Latent exit-code masking.** The pipeline's exit code is now `session-start-record.sh:44`'s unconditional `exit 0`, so a guard's nonzero exit is swallowed. **No behavior change today** — all four guards exit 0 on every path (`goodnight-resume-check.sh:12`; `session-start-checkpoint-reconcile.sh:58,59,60,70,74,82,86,96`; `session-start-ci-baseline.sh:24,25,26,39`; `session-start-retro.sh:24`), so fail-open is preserved. Worth one comment line so a future guard author knows.
- **m5 — Probe not landed.** The `sh`-shim resolution (A3) is a substrate fact that `scripts/tests` now depends on, reported in prose only. `scripts/probes/SubstrateFloor.Probes.Tests.ps1` is exactly where it belongs. (The Copilot-parse probe correctly has nothing to pin — the car's reasoning at `SessionStartWiring.Tests.ps1:12-15` is sound.)
- **m6 — Pre-existing lying canary the car discovered and left.** `scripts/probes/CheckpointReconcile.Probes.Tests.ps1:44-45` claims *"the PowerShell tool's pwsh lacks sh on PATH per docs/friction-log.md 07-23."* Observed false today. Not created by this diff; flagged because the car relied on its falsity and did not land the correction.
- **Fixture-shape nit (no finding):** `ReconcileDispatchRecords.Tests.ps1:78` uses `2026-07-23T09:00:00Z`; the probe actually writes `2026-07-22T14:22:40.433421+00:00` (`subagent-stop-probe.sh:26`, `isoformat()`). Field is echoed only, so no behavioral divergence.

## ADJUDICATIONS

**A1 — Did coverage narrow in substance?** **YES.** M1, proven by injection: 15/15 green with an inline `if…fi` + escaped-JSON `printf` installed on a SessionStart line. The original assertion rejects that string.

**A2 — Second copy vs shared helper; is the pin strong?** **Reasoning sound; pin is STRONG — the strongest in this diff.** The disclosure (`Reconcile-DispatchRecords.ps1:38-56`) is honest and the trade is defensible: `Produce-Artifact.ps1` is the one writer, invoked via `pwsh -File` from latency-sensitive hooks (the 11.6s-vs-2.8s stop-path probe is a real constraint), and the reconciler needs 1 of its 4 branches. **Three fault injections, all reverted byte-identical:**

| Injection | Site | Observed |
|---|---|---|

## NEW MAJOR FINDINGS — all four created by the fixes

### N1 — A second SessionStart within 60s appends to the previous session's report instead of truncating. Three landed surfaces now assert the opposite; one of them was written in this delta.
`.claude/hooks/session-start-record.sh:77` (`STALE_SECONDS=60`), `:101-113`.

Observed:
```
batch 1 → [session-start-report] 2026-07-26T12:50:11Z / BATCH-1-GUARD-A / BATCH-1-GUARD-B
  (3 seconds later, a second SessionStart)
batch 2 → [session-start-report] 2026-07-26T12:50:11Z   &lt;-- previous session's marker
          BATCH-1-GUARD-A / BATCH-1-GUARD-B / BATCH-2-GUARD-A / BATCH-2-GUARD-B
```
A Copilot agent reads a file whose timestamp line belongs to the *previous* session and whose content mixes two sessions, with nothing to tell them apart. Law 1 on the exact surface this feature exists to create.

Falsified claims, all still on disk: `.github/copilot-instructions.md:17` *"freshly truncated per session"*; `ONBOARDING.md:63` *"freshly truncated per session, first line a timestamp marker"*; and `.gitignore:2` *"freshly truncated per session"* — **added by commit `db1d3da` in this very fix cycle.** Round 1's `--reset` handled this case correctly, so this is a regression, and it is nowhere disclosed (I grepped the script, both design amendments, `docs/setup.md`, and both pointer files: no mention of `STALE_SECONDS`' consequence).

Not exotic: this repo's own doctrine enumerates *"a session resumes after compaction"* as a first-class trigger (`ONBOARDING.md:74`, `docs/setup.md:55`, design `:145`). Wall-clock proximity is not session identity, and no value of `STALE_SECONDS` makes it one.

### N2 — The `date -r` fallback the script calls "the safe default" loses 75% of guard output under concurrency.
`.claude/hooks/session-start-record.sh:96-98` (*"an unreadable/missing file or an unparseable result both fall through to 'stale' (reset), the safe default"*), `:102`.

`date -u -r FILE +%s` is GNU-specific. On BSD/macOS `date -r` takes a seconds argument; on busybox it is absent. **Injection E** (simulating that, reverted byte-identical): 4 concurrent guards × 10 trials —

```
RESULT: 30 / 40 guard outputs LOST
last trial's report: [session-start-report] 2026-07-26T12:51:14Z
                     GUARD-C-10
```

Falling through to *stale* on every invocation makes every guard truncate its predecessors; the mutex converts a race into deterministic total loss of all but the last. The "safe default" claim is false — and it is the fallback the whole M4 fix rests on. Round 1's mechanism had no `date` dependency at all. One-word remedy: on an undeterminable mtime, treat as **fresh** (a duplicate append is recoverable; a truncate is not), or use `find "$F" -mmin -1` / an `-nt` comparison instead of asking `date`.

### N3 — The mutex releases a lock it never acquired; the documented bound is 2.5x optimistic.
`.claude/hooks/session-start-record.sh:87-93` — the `trap 'rmdir "$LOCK_DIR"' EXIT` at `:93` is installed unconditionally, on the `break`-out (fail-open) path as well as on the acquire path.

Observed, slow guard vs fast guard:
```
t=1s     lock dir exists? YES   (slow guard holds it, still inside its critical section)
         fast guard wall time: 5126 ms   (header :84-85 documents "bounded (~2s)")
t=~3s    lock dir exists? NO - released by a process that never held it
```
So once any invocation fails open, the mutex is destroyed while the true holder is still running, and a third invocation can enter concurrently. `session-start-record.sh:40-44` asserts *"only one invocation's body runs at a time"* and `docs/setup.md:123-124` asserts the mutex means *"no guard's output can be lost"* — both stronger than the mechanism supports.

This is realistic, not theoretical: the lock is held across `tee`, i.e. for the **upstream guard's entire runtime**, and `session-start-ci-baseline.sh:24-39` makes a network `gh run list` call. Latency measured: leaked-lock batch **6563 ms** vs **515 ms** baseline — against a brief constraint of "trivial latency". *(I did not reproduce data loss through this path and do not claim it; the finding is that the guard's stated mutual-exclusion property is demonstrably false.)*

### N4 — The M5 epoch floor silently defeated the #32 divergence pin. Producer-side drift is no longer caught.
`scripts/tests/ReconcileDispatchRecords.Tests.ps1:199-219` (pin test 1) vs `Reconcile-DispatchRecords.ps1:191-220`.

Round 1, injection C (`Produce-Artifact.ps1:165`, `agent_id` → `session_id`): **8 passed / 1 failed**, the pin red by name. Round 2, same injection: **14 passed / 0 failed.**

Root cause, reproduced by hand under the injection:
```
[reconcile] NOTE - 1 probe firing(s) excluded as pre-producer-epoch (before 2026-07-23T10:00:00+00:00) - expected, not gaps
[reconcile] no gaps - every would-have-adapted probe firing has a matching returned record
EXIT=0
```
The pin's producer runs with `-Now '2026-07-23T10:00:00Z'`, so the derived floor is `10:00`; the pin's probe line is `_probe_logged_at = 2026-07-23T09:00:00Z`, so it is **always** pre-epoch and **always** excluded. The test's two assertions still hold — for entirely the wrong reason. Test 1 is the only test that chains the producer's subject rule to the reconciler's copy; with it vacuous, the deliberate second copy (`Reconcile-DispatchRecords.ps1:112-117`) is unpinned against its source.

The car *did* notice this interaction for the fixture-behaviour tests and moved `gap-agent-123` to `11:00` with a comment explaining why — but a test that breaks announces itself, and a test that goes vacuous stays green. Injections A and B still fire (reconciler-side subject drift: 6 failures; exclusion-rule removal: 2 failures, including the pin's internal-subagent test), so the *exclusion* half survives; the *producer-agreement* half is gone.

Remedy is small: give pin test 1 a post-floor `_probe_logged_at` (or pass `-Since`), plus a red-first test asserting the pin fails under producer drift.

## MINOR FINDINGS

- **N5** — `Reconcile-DispatchRecords.ps1:243` prints *"no gaps - every would-have-adapted probe firing has a matching returned record"* even when firings were excluded and none matched. Observed verbatim in the N4 reproduction: one firing excluded, zero matched, and the line still claims every firing has a record. Law 1; the NOTE line above it does not repair the sentence below it.
- **N6 — the M5 residual: ADJUDICATED, refusal upheld, posture improvable.** The car refused to exclude `adba6552daddbe6db` (the probe log's first line, no `_probe_logged_at`) because it cannot be *proven* pre-epoch. **That refusal is correct** — inventing an exclusion on an assumption is precisely what Law 1 and the severity philosophy forbid, and it is the harder, more honest call. But the car weighed two options where three exist: an undatable entry is not a proven gap *either*, and rendering it as a FLAG (permanent nightly exit 1, unresolvable — no record can ever be written for a firing that predates the producer) asserts "defect" with the same confidence as a real gap. A third disposition — NOTE-severity, *"undatable, first line in the log, presumed pre-epoch, not counted"* — satisfies both laws and leaves the exit code clean. Substantially mitigated by `.claude/skills/goodnight/SKILL.md`, which names the agent_id, explains why it survives, and gives the closure path; a conductor is not misled. Minor, not Major.
- **N7** — Overstated guard claims: `session-start-record.sh:40-44` and `docs/setup.md:123-124`, given N3.
- **N8 (nit)** — `SessionStartReportConcurrency.Tests.ps1` leaks 80+ lines of child-process stdout into the suite and CI logs (observed in every run above); redirect the children's stdout.

**Verified correct, worth stating:** the `returned-*.json` filename scoping (`Reconcile-DispatchRecords.ps1:160`) drops **25 records, all under `artifacts/reviews/`** (hand-landed verdicts) and **zero producer-written records** — 96 `kind:returned` total, 71 producer-named. It matches the producer's own contract: `Produce-Artifact.ps1:425` builds `"$Kind-$compactAt.json"` and `:408` already filters `returned-*.json`. The header's citation to `:394-397` is substantively true but `:425`/`:408` is the sharper coordinate. The second bug the car found is real and correctly fixed.

## CONVERGENCE RULING — CAP SET, ESCALATE

**Direction: the named findings converged; the mechanism did not.** 11/11 round-1 findings Present. Majors 5 → 4 — a nominal decline that means nothing, because the composition inverted: **0 of round 2's Majors are unresolved round-1 findings, and 4 of 4 are defects the round-1 fixes created.**

Swirl triggers (`CLAUDE.md`, Review calibration) — **two of three fire:**
- *Majors not declining* — 5 → 4. Borderline; I do not claim it.
- *Findings cluster in the same section across rounds* — **FIRES.** Round 1: M1 + M4 in the SessionStart delivery mechanism. Round 2: **N1 + N2 + N3 all in `session-start-record.sh`**, one file, one mechanism.
- *A round's findings include defects the previous round's own fixes created* — **FIRES decisively, 4/4.** The doctrine calls this the sharpest signal: *"the defect is being RELOCATED, not resolved."*

This is also the verbatim instrument tell: *"successive revisions close every named finding and open new ones in the SAME section."* Two mechanisms have now been written for one requirement; each was correct against the tests written for it, and each lost guard output under conditions those tests did not model.

**THE CAP: no round 3 on the M4 delivery mechanism.** A third revision of a shell-level mutex in `session-start-record.sh` is predicted to produce a fourth defect in the same file. N4 is *not* under the cap — it is an ordinary regression with a small, clear fix, and should be fixed.

**ESCALATE TO THE OWNER — the premise no round has questioned:** *that `session-start-record.sh` must determine session-batch identity by itself.* Round 1 used array ordering; round 2 used wall-clock mtime. Both are proxies for a fact neither can observe, because the script's stdin is occupied by the guard's output, so it can never read the SessionStart payload that actually carries `session_id` and `source`. Remove that premise and the problem dissolves — a runtime-supplied batch key, or one dedicated hook line owning truncation with guards only ever appending, or per-guard files a reader concatenates (no shared mutable file: no truncation, no race, no mutex, no `date -r`). Every one of those is smaller than what is already written.

**Second escalation, scope:** whether #50 Task 2 should ship before the Copilot leg is OBSERVED at all. The car's own M3 amendment now honestly records that the pipe form is *"an argument … never an OBSERVATION."* Hardening an unobservable mechanism through review rounds spends gate attention on a question no test on this desk can settle.

Fixed by redirecting both streams and draining them asynchronously via `Register-ObjectEvent` (never synchronous-after-`WaitForExit`, which would serialize the concurrency under test; never left undrained, avoiding pipe-buffer deadlock risk), with per-process event unregistration. ## N4 — the strongest item in this round, and it holds up under attack

**My injection C, re-applied:** 15 passed / 2 failed, both by name —
- `a REAL dispatch (stop-car.json): the producer's written record satisfies the reconciler's gap check for the SAME fixture` — the original pin, desilenced by moving its fixture `_probe_logged_at` from `09:00:00Z` (always pre-floor, always excluded) to `11:00:00Z` (post-floor, forced through the actual subject-matching path).
- `PRODUCER-SIDE SUBJECT DRIFT is caught: …` — the new permanent regression test.

*Precision, so a later reader is not misled:* under injection C the new test reds at its **sanity assertion** (`$originalText | Should -Match ([regex]::Escape($needle))`), because the needle no longer exists in the real file. That is correct and desirable — a guard that reds loudly when its anchor moves, rather than silently injecting nothing. The **behavioural** chain is what I exercised separately, below.

**The sibling-copy technique — checked for the illustrating-not-testing failure mode, and it is not one.** The coordinator's specific question was whether the copy could itself drift. It cannot:
- `$originalText = Get-Content $script:ProducerScript -Raw` reads the **real, tracked file fresh at test runtime**. The copy IS the real file's text minus one line; there is no second copy persisted anywhere to drift.
- Two sanity assertions bracket the injection: the needle must still exist verbatim, and the replace must actually have changed something. Both are anti-vacuity guards of exactly the shape the `sweep = dict(hook)` scar demands.
- The assertion is behavioural across two real scripts: the drifted producer writes a record, the **unmodified** reconciler is asked whether that record satisfies the same fixture's probe line, and the test requires a nonzero exit naming `a88e7dadda60940ac`. Nothing is compared to itself.
- The sibling placement is justified and the justification is true: `Produce-Artifact.ps1:65-66` imports `Envelope.psm1` and `Artifact.psm1` via `Join-Path $PSScriptRoot`, and `:287` resolves config via `Split-Path $PSScriptRoot -Parent` — a copy anywhere else would not resolve them.

**My own non-vacuity probe of the new test** (reverted byte-identical, test file SHA `3DE714FB…0377D`): I neutered its injection to a **cosmetic-only** change — still `'agent_id'`, only the trailing comment differs. Result: **16 passed / 1 FAILED BY NAME**, `PRODUCER-SIDE SUBJECT DRIFT is caught`. So the test's verdict is bound to the drifted producer's *behaviour*, not to the fact that a textual edit occurred. That is the property that matters, and it is proven.

**Cleanup verified empirically:** zero `Produce-Artifact-drifted-test-copy-*.ps1` left in `scripts/` after 8+ suite runs; `git status --porcelain` empty throughout.

|---|---|---|
| (1) all matched, nothing excluded | 0 | `no gaps - every would-have-adapted probe firing has a matching returned record` — **true** |
| (2) 1 excluded + 1 matched | 0 | `no gaps - every NON-EXCLUDED … has a matching returned record (1 excluded as pre-producer-epoch, see NOTE above)` — **true** |
| (3) all excluded, none matched | 0 | same qualified form — **true**, and it no longer claims what r2 caught it claiming |
| (4) real gap | **1** | `GAP - agent_id=gap1 logged_at=… missing_kind=returned` — **unchanged**, no summary line |

Real-corpus spot-check (read-only, shared checkout): **EXIT=1, 8 excluded as pre-producer-epoch (floor `2026-07-22T16:40:01+00:00`), 1 gap `adba6552daddbe6db logged_at=unknown`** — identical to round 2. The N5 edit did not disturb the epoch floor, the gap path, or the `unknown` rendering (m3).

## N8 — leak gone, and the test did not lose its teeth

Two full `scripts/tests` runs: **no child-process output at all**. (The remaining console noise — `Landed landed.md`, `Migrate-Verdicts:`, `Produce-Artifact:` — comes from other test files and predates round 1.)

**Construction checked for the serialization trap** (`SessionStartReportConcurrency.Tests.ps1:58-81`): all N processes are started and their async drains armed inside the first `foreach` (`:62-78`); `WaitForExit` happens only in a **second** loop (`:79`). No synchronous `ReadToEnd`. So the redirection cannot have serialized the children.

**Proven, not inferred.** I neutered the mutex (`LOCK_DIR="$REPORT_FILE.lock.$$"`, unique per process — a **test-power probe only, not a re-rule of the capped mechanism**, reverted byte-identical, SHA `1C411F37…FAD2`): the concurrency suite went **2 passed / 1 FAILED BY NAME** — `N genuinely concurrent invocations against a STALE/absent file lose NOTHING, across many trials`, with the observed loss count reported in the failure message. Had the N8 redirection serialized the children, that test would have stayed green. It went red. Concurrency is intact.

## NIT (new, in a touched file)

- **N9** — `SessionStartReportConcurrency.Tests.ps1:72-73, 80`: `Unregister-Event` removes the event *subscriber* but leaves the `PSEventJob` in the job table. Measured: subscribers `0 → 0` ✓, but jobs `0 → 166` after one run and `→ 332` after two — linear accumulation of 166 `Stopped` PSEventJobs per run (83 processes × 2 streams). The header's claim at `:56-57` is literally true as written (*"Event subscriptions are unregistered … so they never accumulate"*) — subscriptions do not; the jobs do. No gate is affected: nothing under `scripts/` reads the job table (`git grep` for `Get-Job|Receive-Job|Wait-Job` → none), and the CI host process is short-lived. It matters only to a conductor running the suite repeatedly in one pwsh session. One-token remedy: `Remove-Job -Name $sub.Name -Force -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` alongside the existing `Unregister-Event`.
- *Not raised as a finding, recorded for the record:* the new test writes its throwaway producer copy into the tracked `scripts/` directory. `finally` covers assertion failures (the common case) and I observed zero strays across 8+ runs; a hard process kill could leave one untracked file that `git status` would surface. No gate globs `scripts/*.ps1`.

**On the car's "13/14" claim for injection C:** I observe 15/2 at HEAD. Both are consistent and neither is wrong — the car measured at a point when the file held 14 tests (13 pass / 1 fail, the pin), before its own new regression test landed. At HEAD there are 17. Sequentially coherent; I confirm the substance (the pin fails by name again) and the direction.

## DISCLOSURE FIRST — a process error of mine, self-reported

My first attempt at injection D wrote to **the shared checkout**, not my worktree. Cause: `[System.IO.File]::*` resolves relative paths against `[Environment]::CurrentDirectory` (`C:\Users\Chris\git\starcar`), which PowerShell's `Set-Location` does **not** change. So `.claude/settings.json` was transiently modified in `C:\Users\Chris\git\starcar` for the duration of one Pester run, then restored from the pristine byte array.

**Restoration proven at the git object level, not by feel:**

```
working-file blob: eef387fc954a32387e13a6045992fbd463a50f9d
HEAD blob       : eef387fc954a32387e13a6045992fbd463a50f9d
IDENTICAL       : True
git status --porcelain  -&gt;  CLEAN (zero entries)
```

Nothing was committed or pushed anywhere. I re-ran the injection correctly with absolute paths (results below). This also cost me one wasted injection whose "9/9 green" result was **meaningless** — the worktree file was never injected — and I discarded it rather than reporting it. Worth a friction-log line as a class: *pwsh .NET static file APIs ignore `Set-Location`.*

## MAJOR FINDINGS

### R4-1 — The new test asserts a guard is "always-emitting" when it is conditional. The suite reds under detached HEAD, which is this repo's own PR-CI checkout state on both matrix legs.

`scripts/tests/SessionStartGuardRunner.Tests.ps1:60-63` (the test and its `Should -Match '\[ci-baseline\]'`) vs `.claude/hooks/session-start-ci-baseline.sh:24-26`:

```sh
24: command -v gh &gt;/dev/null 2&gt;&amp;1 || exit 0
25: branch=$(git branch --show-current 2&gt;/dev/null) || exit 0
26: [ -z "$branch" ] &amp;&amp; exit 0
28: echo "[ci-baseline] Branch '$branch' - latest CI conclusions ..."
```

The `[ci-baseline]` marker is emitted **only** if `gh` is present *and* `git branch --show-current` is non-empty. In a detached worktree it is empty, so the guard exits silently at `:26`.

**Observed in this worktree:**
```
branch=[]
sh .claude/hooks/session-start-ci-baseline.sh  -&gt;  (no output)  EXIT=0
command -v gh -&gt; /c/Program Files/GitHub CLI/gh     (so gh is NOT the cause)
```

**This is not a quirk of my box. Observed in this repo's own CI logs:**

- **PR event, run `30114169185`** (PR #51), `actions/checkout@v4`, **both** matrix legs:
  ```
  [command] git checkout --progress --force refs/remotes/pull/51/merge
  Note: switching to 'refs/remotes/pull/51/merge'.
  You are in 'detached HEAD' state.
  ```
- **Push event, run `30204032050`**, for contrast:
  ```
  [command] /usr/bin/git checkout --progress --force -B dev refs/remotes/origin/dev
  Switched to a new branch 'dev'
  ```

`.github/workflows/ci.yml:225-227` runs `Invoke-Pester -Path ./scripts/tests` and `if ($result.FailedCount -gt 0) { exit 1 }`.

**Net failure mode, stated precisely: this test is GREEN on every `dev` push and RED on the pull request that merges the train to `main`** — on both `windows-latest` and `ubuntu-latest`. The merge north star ("never PR or merge except from a good known working state") is not satisfiable from this commit.

Secondary, and it is why no reviewer in this shop can clear it either: every brief in this train mandates a **detached** worktree, and rounds 1-3 all ran detached. This test fails by construction for its own gate.

The test's *name* is the falsehood: `[retro]` is the only unconditional guard of the four (both branches of `session-start-retro.sh:13-23` echo). `[ci-baseline]`, `[checkpoint]` and `[goodnight]` are all conditional. *Remedy is small — assert on the unconditional guard, or gate the ci-baseline assertion on a non-empty `git branch --show-current`.*

### R4-2 — The agent-pull runner delivers a **confident falsehood** to the agent, on the one surface this whole feature exists to create. Law 1, same class as round 2's N1.

`.claude/hooks/run-session-start-guards.sh:43` establishes `GUARD_DIR="${GUARD_DIR:-.claude/hooks}"` but **not** `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`, on which `.claude/hooks/session-start-retro.sh:10` depends:

```sh
LOG="$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/docs/friction-log.md"
```

Run exactly as the arrival docs instruct, from a shell, `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` is unset, `LOG` resolves to `/docs/friction-log.md`, and the `else` branch fires. **Observed, verbatim:**

```
CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR in an agent Bash shell = []
docs/friction-log.md entries actually present: 61

$ sh .claude/hooks/run-session-start-guards.sh
[retro] STANDING ITEM: open this session with the tooling/harness friction retro.
[retro] No docs/friction-log.md yet - create it and log friction AS IT HAPPENS.
[retro] A retro that runs on memory is a memory test.
```

The same guard, with the variable set:

```
$ CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="$(pwd)" sh .claude/hooks/session-start-retro.sh
[retro] docs/friction-log.md holds 61 logged entries.
```

Three things make this Major rather than cosmetic:

1. **It is 100% reproducible in every agent-pull invocation.** `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` is a Claude Code-injected variable; the runner's own header at `:23-24` names "a human operator or a probe" as intended users, and a **Copilot** session — the entire target audience — will not have it. I observed the falsehood even from inside Claude Code, because an agent's Bash shell does not carry it either.
2. **The instruction it emits is destructive-adjacent.** It tells the arriving agent to *create* `docs/friction-log.md`, a file holding 61 entries — on a repo whose doctrine makes the friction log the standing first item of every session.
3. **The deleted mechanism had already fixed this exact class.** Round 1's finding m2 was a bare unanchored path in the delivery script; round 2 fixed it at `session-start-record.sh:76`; that file is now deleted and the replacement re-introduces the class one file over.

*Remedy is one line in the runner:* `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2&gt;/dev/null || echo .)}"` and export it — the same override-with-default shape the runner already uses for `GUARD_DIR`.

**Common root cause of both Majors, and it is one sentence:** the runner's real-guard behaviour was only ever observed inside the car's own attached, Claude-Code-flavoured worktree — never in either environment the feature targets (a detached CI/reviewer checkout; a plain non-Claude-Code shell). The 5/5 green was environment-local, and that is precisely what "claims are never terminal until a second party re-derives them" exists to catch.

## CONVERGENCE RULING — NOT CONVERGED. NOT MERGEABLE FROM THIS COMMIT. **NO NEW CAP.**

Trajectory across the series: **5M/6m → 4M/4m (all fix-created, cap + escalation) → 0M/0m (narrow) → 2M/2m (both fix-created).**

Applying round 2's own swirl framework, honestly, **two of three fire**:

- *Majors not declining* — 5 → 4 → 0 → 2. Ambiguous (r3 was scope-narrow by design). **I do not claim it.**
- *Findings cluster in the same section across rounds* — **FIRES.** r1's M1+M4, r2's N1+N2+N3, and r4's R4-1+R4-2 all sit in "the thing that delivers guard output to a Copilot session," three rounds out of four.
- *A round's findings include defects the previous round's fixes created* — **FIRES**, 2/2.

**And yet I rule this is NOT the swirl state, and I decline to set a cap.** Naming the difference precisely, because getting this wrong in either direction is expensive:

1. **The escalation already ran and worked.** The r2 reviewer capped a *third revision of a shell-level mtime+mutex* and escalated the premise. The owner removed the premise. Round 4 is not that revision — it is a **7-line `for` loop with no shared file, no truncation, no race, no mutex**. Every defect class r2 predicted would recur is structurally absent. I re-tested for them: concurrency is meaningless here, and my missing-guard/middle-failure injections found the non-fatal shape sound.
2. **Both Majors have one mechanical root cause with one mechanical remedy**, not a design that keeps relocating: *the new code was never run in the environment it targets.* Two one-line fixes (export `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`; make the ci-baseline assertion honest) plus a temp-dir `finally` close all four findings.
3. **Capping a 7-line script would be the autoimmune failure** this repo names explicitly — ceremony scaled to the train's history rather than to the artifact in front of me.

**Binding acceptance condition for round 5, since these two defects were both invisible from the author's own worktree:** the fix must be verified by (a) running `scripts/tests` **in a detached-HEAD checkout** and reporting the count observed there, and (b) running the runner in a shell with `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` **unset** and quoting the `[retro]` line verbatim. Green in an attached worktree is not evidence for either finding.

**Credit where it is owed, unhedged.** The owner's ruling was implemented completely and honestly: the deletion is total, the settings revert is byte-exact against base, the amendment history is genuinely append-only with supersession stated rather than implied, the #55 carrier is real and its body matches, and nothing anywhere claims the Copilot leg verified. The r1 whitelist class and the r3 divergence pin both survived and both fired under my injections. That is four rungs of carrier discipline holding.

## R4-1 — CLOSED. And I ruled on the skip rather than accepting it.

`scripts/tests/SessionStartGuardRunner.Tests.ps1:69-79` (unconditional `[retro]`) and `:81-96` (conditional `[ci-baseline]`).

**Is skip-with-reason the right disposition versus a conditional assertion? YES, and it is the honest one.** Four alternatives, ruled:

1. **Assert unconditionally** — that is R4-1 verbatim. Rejected already.
2. **Silent conditional** (`if (attached) { assert }`) — the test would report **PASS while asserting nothing**, in the exact environment CI runs in. This repo's own CI header calls that out: *"a green light wired to nothing is worse than no light."* Strictly worse than a skip.
3. **Delete the coverage** — loses a real assertion that holds and runs on push-event CI.
4. **Skip with a stated reason** — the run output names the precondition and why it was not met. That is *unknown renders as unknown* (Law 1) applied to a test result. **Correct.**

**Is the skip masking a broken assertion? NO — I proved the positive branch.** I temporarily attached a throwaway branch at the identical commit (disclosed below) and ran the file:

```
ATTACHED: Passed=7 Failed=0 Skipped=0 Total=7
PASSED  : running the REAL guards prints [ci-baseline]'s marker ONLY when attached to a branch ...
```

The assertion executes and holds when its precondition is met. A skip that could never pass would be a finding; this one passes.

**Is the reason text's PR-CI parity claim accurate? YES, observed in round 4** — CI run `30114169185` (PR #51) logs `You are in 'detached HEAD' state.` on **both** matrix legs, while push run `30204032050` logs `-B dev` / `Switched to a new branch 'dev'`. The text says exactly that.

**Does a skip pass the CI gate? YES — traced, not assumed.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:225-233` gates on **only** two things:

```powershell
if ($result.FailedCount -gt 0) { exit 1 }
if ($result.PassedCount -eq 0) { ...exit 1 }
```

A skip is neither a failure nor a zero-pass. Detached: `FailedCount=0`, `PassedCount=181`. **The merge PR goes green.** No `-Strict`, no skip-as-failure setting anywhere in the workflow (grepped).

**Disclosed coverage boundary, not a defect:** the `[ci-baseline]` positive assertion never executes on PR-event CI. It does execute on push-to-`main` and push-to-`dev` CI (attached, confirmed) and on any attached local run. Coverage is retained where it *can* run, and its absence is announced rather than hidden.

## R4-2 — CLOSED. The falsehood is gone; I ran the acceptance command myself.

`.claude/hooks/run-session-start-guards.sh:59-61`:

```sh
CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel 2&gt;/dev/null || echo .)}"
export CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR

Ground truth `grep -c` on the real file: **61**. The count is correct, and the round-4 falsehood (`No docs/friction-log.md yet - create it`) is **absent** (tested explicitly).

**Fallback chain — asked, tested, and it degrades LOUDLY.** From a temp dir outside any git repo, `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` exits **128**, the `|| echo .` fallback fires, and:

```
sh: ./.claude/hooks/goodnight-resume-check.sh: No such file or directory
sh: ./.claude/hooks/session-start-checkpoint-reconcile.sh: No such file or directory
sh: ./.claude/hooks/session-start-ci-baseline.sh: No such file or directory
sh: ./.claude/hooks/session-start-retro.sh: No such file or directory
RUNNER_EXIT=0
```

Four explicit lines naming every path tried. Nothing is silently lost (Law 4) — the failure is unmistakable and cannot be read as "the guards ran and had nothing to say." `exit 0` is consistent with the runner's documented always-non-fatal contract (`:33-37`). **Honest degradation. Not a finding.**

Two properties I checked because they are the ways this fix could have been subtly wrong:
- `${VAR:-default}` (not `${VAR-default}`) — an **empty** `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` is correctly replaced, not passed through.
- The export lives in the child `sh` process only; the caller's environment is untouched. `sh "$GUARD_DIR/$guard"` stays quoted, so a repo path with spaces is safe.

**Non-vacuity — I watched the new guard fire.** Injection F: I reverted the three lines to the round-4 shape.

```
INJECTED (fix neutered): Passed=5 Failed=1 Skipped=1
FAILED BY NAME: CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR unset: the retro guard reports the real friction-log
                entry count, never the false "create it" line (#50, R4-2)
```

Reverted byte-identical: SHA-256 `F454FCDC27D8C06BDFFAF789AE4504373634CEBD7D6088D63C7BBEC46904786D` before and after, `git status` empty.

The test also asserts `holds \d+ logged entries` rather than hardcoding `61` — no second copy of a mutable count to go stale (Law 6).

## The doc sentence check: every claim traced, hop by hop

The new sentence (`schema/vectors/adapter/README.md:94-100`) makes four technical claims plus one carrier tag. All five resolve.

### Trace A - "the producer's envelope-resolution code (`Get-LastAssistantText`/`Get-StarcarEnvelope` deriving `subject`/`task_id` from a fenced `task-id`)" - **TRUE**

1. Prose: `schema/vectors/adapter/README.md:94-96`.
2. `Get-LastAssistantText` exists: `scripts/Envelope.psm1:29`, exported `:159`.
3. `Get-StarcarEnvelope` exists: `scripts/Envelope.psm1:86`, exported `:159`.
4. Fenced-`task-id` derivation is real: fence regex `scripts/Envelope.psm1:112` (`^[ \t]*```starcar-artifact`), field parse `:127` (`^(task-id|outcome|findings|abstract):`), `$taskId` assigned `:138`, surfaced as `TaskId` `:154` - and non-required by design `:143-147`.
5. The producer's **returned** path calls both: `scripts/Produce-Artifact.ps1:217` (`Get-LastAssistantText`), `:237` (`Get-StarcarEnvelope`).
6. `task_id` derived: `Produce-Artifact.ps1:243` (`$taskId = $env.TaskId`) then written `:358`.
7. `subject` derived from that same task-id, for this exact runtime/kind pair: `Produce-Artifact.ps1:259-263` (`if ($runtime -eq 'copilot' -and [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($subject)) { ... $subject = $taskId }`), written `:337`.
8. Behavior observed, not inferred: `AdapterVectors.Tests.ps1` case `conforms: copilot-stop-envelope-taskid` **passed** in my run, and the vector asserts `subject: "51-car-r1"` equal to `task_id: "51-car-r1"`.

### Trace B - "exercised against a Claude-format transcript fixture wrapped in a Copilot-shaped payload" - **TRUE**

1. Payload wrapper is Copilot-shaped: `schema/vectors/adapter/copilot-stop-envelope-taskid.json:9-12` carries `agent_name` + snake_case `transcript_path`, with **no** `agent_type` and **no** `agent_id`.
2. That is precisely the shape the producer's Copilot branch recognises: `Produce-Artifact.ps1:162` (`agent_name` reads as the Copilot compat stop key) leading to `:166-172` (`runtime = 'copilot'`, `subject = $null`), and `:213` (the `transcript_path` fallback after `agent_transcript_path` misses).
3. Transcript lines are Claude-jsonl-shaped: same vector `:15-16`, `{"type":"assistant","message":{"role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":...}]}}`.
4. That is exactly the shape `Get-LastAssistantText` consumes: `scripts/Envelope.psm1:57-58` (`message.role -eq 'assistant'`) and `:69-72` (`content[].type -eq 'text'`).
5. It is shape-identical to the in-repo OBSERVED Claude fixture `scripts/tests/fixtures/payloads/transcript-car.jsonl:2,4` and to the Claude vector `schema/vectors/adapter/claude-stop-envelope-taskid.json:11-12`. So "Claude-format... wrapped in a Copilot-shaped payload" is literally accurate on both halves.

### Trace C - "a real Copilot background stop's `transcript_path` points at the parent's `events.jsonl`, not a per-agent Claude-format transcript (§3b-6/3b-7)" - **TRUE** (see MINOR-1 on completeness)

1. Prose: README.md:97-99.
2. **§3b-7 resolves and says exactly this**, verbatim at `docs/design/2026-07-24-dual-runtime-harness-design.md:97-100`: *"**`transcript_path` at stop points at the PARENT's events.jsonl**, not a per-agent file (probe-log entries; ...)"*. It is item 7 of the §3b numbered list, so the `§3b-7` reference resolves correctly.

No live-design line contradicts §3b-6 or §3b-7.

### Trace D - "the real Copilot envelope round-trip always flows through the events.jsonl branch this note already defers below" - **TRUE**

1. **The cross-reference is true**: the note below does defer that branch - `README.md:103-106`, *"That events.jsonl extraction branch is NOT pinned by a vector here... It is deferred as named work, not silently skipped."* The sentence's own pointer resolves.

It contains exactly two functions, `Read-TranscriptLines:20` and `Read-JsonlObjects:41`, neither of which detects or interprets a record shape; `:15-18` states outright that *"interpreting an events.jsonl's per-runtime record SHAPE is deliberately out of scope here."* The car's claim reproduces.
3. **And none exists anywhere else**: `git grep -n "execution_start|execution_complete|subagent.started|TranscriptFormat|copilot-events" -- scripts/` returns **zero hits**. The deferral is real repo-wide, not just in one file.
4. **Why "always" holds**: the branch's design definition names both call sites - superseded design `:126` (D5), *"`Get-LastAssistantText` grows an events.jsonl format branch... 5. **What a real Copilot stop does today, traced end to end** (confirming the branch is genuinely required and the README is not describing something that exists): `Envelope.psm1:79-81` finds no assistant message and returns one error, `Produce-Artifact.ps1:222-235` classifies that as a transcript read failure (outcome `error`), then `:259-262` throws *"Copilot returned payload carries no envelope task-id to pair on"* - exit 1, fault logged, **no record**. The sentence claims no working real path, and none exists.

`artifacts/reviews/REDACTED.md:76` carries the heading *"CR-3 (NEW - MINOR, carried, non-blocking) - README overstates real-runtime coverage of the Copilot stop-with-envelope path"*; `:77` states the evidence and quotes §3b-6 and §3b-7; `:84` prescribes the fix: *"clarify that the vector pins the producer's envelope-resolution code given a readable Claude-format transcript, and that for a real Copilot background stop the envelope arrives via events.jsonl (§3b-6/7)."* The landed sentence covers **both** clauses. # VERDICT: **CONFIRM** — the hotfix stands

The Minors are accuracy defects in the *disclosure and the friction-log framing*, plus two surviving siblings of the class — none of which is grounds to force rework-forward on this diff.

## Leg-by-leg boundary ruling

Tests Passed: 181, Failed: 1
```

**(b) Mechanical, coverage-class — CONFIRMED.** 2 files, +26/−2. `scripts/tests/SessionStartWiring.Tests.ps1` (helper bodies inside `BeforeAll` only) and `docs/friction-log.md`. **Zero product code; zero assertions changed; the hook under test is untouched.** That last point is the important one: the fix corrects the test's *ability to observe*, not the behavior being observed.

**(c) Red for the stated reason — CONFIRMED, and independently re-derived.** I did not take the commit message's word for the causal chain. This box has WSL Ubuntu-24.04, so I isolated each defect on real Linux (script at `C:\Users\Chris\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-Chris-git-starcar\15c84ef1-8e84-4d98-aacd-17cce34752cb\scratchpad\probe.sh`):

and case 2b produced `STUB_ENTIRE_CALLED_WITH: hooks claude-code session-start`. **Ruling: leg (c)'s spirit is satisfied** — the red is a genuine observed failure, for the stated reason, now confirmed by a second party who ran it. ## The fix itself

- `[System.IO.Path]::PathSeparator` — correct. Observed `';'` on this box (pwsh 7.6.3); `':'` on Linux is proven by the green ubuntu leg of run 30206223085. **On Windows the fixed line is byte-equivalent in effect to the old one** (`PathSeparator == ';'`), so Git-bash's Windows-PATH translation is unchanged — confirmed by injection A passing 9/9.
- `if (-not $IsWindows) { &amp; chmod +x $stubScript }` (`SessionStartWiring.Tests.ps1:150`) — correct. `$IsWindows` is a pwsh-6+ automatic `Boolean` (observed `True`, type `Boolean`). Unchecked `chmod` exit is not a silent-loss risk: a failed chmod surfaces as the absent-branch assertion failure.
- `[regex]::Escape($sep)` — observed identity for both `';'` and `':'`. Harmless and defensive, not a defect.

**Fault injection (check 3).** Restored the hardcoded `';'` at `SessionStartWiring.Tests.ps1:153`, confirmed the injection landed via `git diff`, ran on Windows: **9 passed / 0 failed**. Reverted byte-identically (SHA-256 back to `C2B4CCEE032207D9A5F5A9108120E46673AE0DF7CB68E1E7484C630F1147D58D`, `git status` empty). Combined with WSL case 3, this **validates the friction-log's class claim**: the defect is Windows-invisible and Linux-fatal. Corroborated by CI itself — at 42e3eae with both defects present, `verify (windows-latest)` was **success**.

**Suite counts observed by me**, worktree `81df6cc`, Windows, pwsh 7.6.3:
- `scripts/tests` detached-worktree cwd: **181 passed / 0 failed / 1 skipped / 182 total** (twice)
- `scripts/tests` attached cwd: **182 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped / 182 total**
- The skip is `run-session-start-guards.sh: agent-pull runner (#50).running the REAL guards prints [ci-baseline]'s marker ONLY when attached to a branch` — pre-existing conditional design, not this hotfix's concern, exactly as the brief stated.

## Findings

**F1 — Minor (ticket required): two surviving instances of the class this commit names.**

- `C:\Users\Chris\git\starcar-wt\review-hotfix-stub\scripts\probes\SubstrateFloor.Probes.Tests.ps1:98` — `($env:PATH -split ';' | ...) -join ';'`. **Vacuous green on ubuntu, proven not asserted**: fed a colon-separated PATH, the split yields **1 element**, the filter keeps it, the join reconstitutes the input — `IDENTITY: True`. And it reports green on the leg: run 30206223085, `verify (ubuntu-latest)`, `[+] `sh` still resolves with scoop\shims stripped from PATH`. Nothing was stripped. This **falsifies issue #14's own thesis** ("The substrate-floor probe suite either passes on Linux or names exactly what breaks") — it passes on Linux having tested nothing, which is the lying-instrument shape `ci.yml`'s zero-test refusals exist to reject.
- `C:\Users\Chris\git\starcar-wt\review-hotfix-stub\scripts\tests\LandVerdictRider.Tests.ps1:78` — same shape, **less severe**: its self-validating guard at `:83` (`probe invalid: entire still resolvable`, exit 9) converts the latent defect into a loud failure rather than a false green. A correct no-op on CI today.

**Why this is not a Major against this diff, stated so the call is auditable:** both defects pre-date `81df6cc`; the diff neither created nor worsened them; and fixing them inside the hotfix would have broken leg (a) (not CI-blocking) and leg (b) (scope). **The hotfix was right to stay narrow.** But the friction-log entry landed *in this same commit* states the rule universally, and no open issue covers it (checked all 30 open; #14 is closest and is about landing the leg, not auditing existing tests). The rule is normative ("must use"), not descriptive, so this is an open obligation rather than a stale document — hence not a Major on that axis either. **Recommend the mechanical form, whose prior art is already in-repo:** `docs/templates/repo-policy-check-patterns.md` §1, instantiated as `scripts/tests/DocPolicy.Tests.ps1` — a repo-policy gate rejecting hardcoded `';'` PATH splits under `scripts/` is a known shape, not an invention.

**F2 — Minor: the friction-log framing overstates environmental inevitability, and that forecloses the lesson that would have caught F1.**

`docs/friction-log.md:219` headline "THE UBUNTU LEG CAUGHT WHAT NO DESK REVIEW COULD" and `:224` "Five review rounds (two reviewers, both on this Windows box) could not have seen it."

Every *mechanical* claim in that entry checks out — I verified each one, including "five rounds, two reviewers" (verdicts round1–round5 exist; round4 is the rotation). This claim does not. A hardcoded `';'` is a **structural** fact, and CLAUDE.md's own probe doctrine draws exactly that line: structural claims are settled by opening the file, behavioural ones only by running. Direct counter-evidence: `artifacts/reviews/2026-07-26-tooling-50-32-review-round1-REJECT.md:180` (§A3) examined **this very PATH-stripping technique for CI portability** and concluded "NO portability regression … that test's PATH-stripping technique is sound. … No finding." A reviewer reached this code, reasoned about its cross-OS behavior, and cleared it. That is *looked at it and missed it*, not *could not have seen it*.

This is consequence, not pedantry: if desk review structurally could not catch the class, no reading check gets added and F1's two siblings stay invisible. Suggested correction — keep the class lesson verbatim, replace the counterfactual with the checkable fact: *"a Windows-only RUN could not exhibit it; desk review could have, and round 1 §A3 examined this exact helper for CI portability and cleared it."*

**F3 — Minor: the commit's leg-(c) disclosure contains a false clause.** "red observed for the stated reason in **the only environment that can exhibit it**." False as stated: WSL Ubuntu-24.04 is installed on this box and reproduces the failure end-to-end in one command, including the byte-exact fallback JSON. The leg is still satisfied — the CI red is genuine — but the probe doctrine prefers the local run, and it was one `wsl` away. Same shape as F2: both overstate that the environment made the miss unavoidable.

**F4 — Minor: verification-honesty coordinate missing.** "local Windows suite re-run green 182/182 post-fix" omits attached-vs-detached. I reconciled both numbers (above) and **the claim is true for the conductor's environment**. Raised because the precedent is explicit and two days old: `artifacts/reviews/REDACTED.md:274` graded exactly this omission a verification-honesty **VIOLATION** ("The car claimed `scripts/tests 180/180`; a second party re-deriving observes **179/1/180**").

## My own process violation (disclosed, not softened)

**My first fault injection wrote to the SHARED CHECKOUT, not the worktree.** `[System.IO.File]::ReadAllText/WriteAllText` resolve relative paths against `[Environment]::CurrentDirectory`, which PowerShell's `Set-Location` does not change. What exposed it: the "injected" hash equalled the pre-injection hash (`C2B4CCEE...`), impossible if content had changed. Restored byte-identically from a pre-injection byte backup (`C59BAE...` → `C2B4CCEE...`); `git -C C:\Users\Chris\git\starcar status --short` and `git diff --stat` both empty. **That round's result (9/9) was discarded as meaningless** — the worktree file was never modified, so Pester had run un-injected code. The injection was redone with absolute paths, with the worktree `git diff` shown as proof it landed.

Aggravating, and it is evidence for F1's remedy: `docs/friction-log.md:198-206` records **this exact mistake**, made by the round-4 reviewer, dated 2026-07-26 — in the very file I was reviewing. I am the second reviewer in two days to make it. The lesson landed as prose only, and prose did not bind the next new-hire-on-day-one.

## MAJOR findings

### MAJOR-1 — Design **Rule 4** and the gating matrix that quotes it were invalidated by `9e5261f` and left stale

`docs/design/2026-07-21-v0-yard-skeleton-design.md:184-185`:

&gt; **Rule 4:** the detector's register is `needs-attention`, deliberately - the one alarm that is about the board rather than the yard.

`docs/contracts/gating-matrix.md:45` quotes it verbatim as the reason the detector surface is never suppressed.

`9e5261f` made both false: `board/store/condition_severity.go:42` declares `"discovery": TierNote`, and `RegisterForCode` (`:92-97`) returns `"nominal"` for it. Observed on the wire in my own live run: `{"code":"discovery","detail":"outcome: totally-made-up-outcome","register":"nominal"}`.

The car's §12b amendment (`:473-490`) names and supersedes **§6's row 335 only**. Neither `docs/contracts/gating-matrix.md` nor §5.2 appears in any of the three commits' file lists (`git show --stat 9e5261f b9595b9 8658011`). Rule 4 is the *composition rule* the whole view is built on, and the gating matrix is a named contract document — a stale one is a lying canary on a truth surface, and this is the North Star rule verbatim: the commit that invalidates a document updates it in the same commit.

Aggravating: the gating-matrix row's rationale exists specifically to say this alarm is *never softened*; post-#30 a discovery is calm **and** collapsed behind chrome by default. Whether that is right is settled (the owner ruled it), but the contract that says otherwise cannot be left on the page.

### MAJOR-2 — §12b over-claims its supersession: `position`/`role` did **not** change register

`docs/design/2026-07-21-v0-yard-skeleton-design.md:474-479` quotes §6's row in full — which reads "Unrecognised `kind`/`outcome`/**`position`/role**" — and then rules: "NOTE-tier renders `nominal`, not `needs-attention` … only the register changes."

For `position` and `role` that is **false**:
- `board/fold/algorithm.go:110-122` mints discoveries **only** for unrecognised `kind` and `outcome`. An unrecognised position or role produces no board condition at all, so `condition_severity.go` cannot possibly have changed it.
- The register for those still resolves `needs-attention` at `board/web/js/vocab.js:36`, reached via `board/web/js/compose.js:31` `positionRegister` and `board/web/js/render.js:155`. Pinned green at HEAD, observed in my run: TAP `ok 70` — *"describeVocab: an UNRECOGNISED id renders its raw id verbatim as the label, register needs-attention, never guessed as calm."*

A reader taking the supersession at face value concludes unrecognised positions now render calm. They do not.

### MAJOR-3 — The mockup-brief amendment asserts CALM for a path that is still HOT, and orphans an in-code citation

`docs/design/2026-07-23-ui-mockup-brief.md:87-96`. The diff **deleted the word "hot"** from a landed, still-true claim and appended: *"a 'discovery' is NOTE-tier … and therefore renders CALM, not hot - the state word this bullet's own example uses (an unrecognised dispatch STATE, not an outcome) is a different board-condition path (fold-fault/discovery via `board/server/poll.go`)."*

Both halves are wrong:
1. An unrecognised **dispatch state word is not a fold discovery at all** (`board/fold/algorithm.go:110-122` — kind and outcome only). Its path is `board/web/js/render.js:185` → `board/web/js/vocab.js:32-36`, entirely view-side, untouched by this diff.
2. It still renders **hot**, pinned green at HEAD and observed by me: `board/web/test/render.test.js:217` `assert.equal(item.stateRegister, 'needs-attention')` — TAP `ok 55`, *"discovery rendering: an unrecognised dispatch state word renders HOT, BY NAME, VERBATIM."* The car left that test untouched and passing while writing the opposite into the design doc.

Orphaned citation in the same class, left stale by the same commit — `board/web/js/vocab.js:9-11`:
&gt; `// rev 5 S5.2, mockup brief "the discovery state") renders it hot, BY NAME, verbatim`

That comment cites the exact bullet whose "hot" was deleted. The code (`vocab.js:36`) is right; the design doc is the thing that is now wrong, and the citation no longer resolves.

*Premise check on the brief's framing:* the brief called this "append-only house style." I checked — there is **no** append-only rule for design docs in this repo (`append-only` appears only for the artifact store and the friction log; the sole prior amendment precedent is `2026-07-21-v0-yard-skeleton-design.md:462`). So the *inline* placement is not itself a violation and I am not charging it. The **deletion of a true word** and the **false replacement claim** are the finding.

## Minor findings

**MIN-1 — the two new outcome defs sit outside the pin that protects their eight siblings.** `scripts/tests/BoardDefs.Tests.ps1:41-62`'s `$script:PinnedRegisters.outcomes` table is a **subset** check, so `completed`/`approve-for-merge` are the only outcomes in `schema/vocab/board-defs.json` with no by-name register pin. **Fault-injected and proven:** flipping `completed` to `needs-attention` in the committed file → `BoardDefs.Tests.ps1` still **11/11 passed, 0 failed** (reverted byte-identical, `bd642be` → `bd642be`). Contrast the same file's own MANDATORY non-vacuity test, which proves a flipped `REJECT` *is* caught by name. Compounding: `board-defs.json:2`'s `$comment` opens "THE REGISTER ASSIGNMENTS ARE PINNED (plan task 2.4…)" and the car's appended sentence does not say these two are the exception. Two lines in the pinned table closes it.

**MIN-2 — the AST scanner's skip is broader than its stated intent.** `board/store/condition_severity_test.go:55-57` skips **every** `*ast.SelectorExpr` Code value; the doc comment (`:40-43`) justifies it narrowly as "a simple field-selector pass-through (`Code: c.Code`)". **Fault-injected and proven:** I added a genuinely new code supplied as a qualified selector — `WireBoardCondition{Code: time.RFC3339, …}` in `board/server/poll.go` — and `TestConditionSeverityMappingMatchesEmittedCodes` printed **`ok`**, silently blind (reverted byte-identical, `1ada3d4` → `1ada3d4`). Harm is bounded (`RegisterForCode`'s Law 1 default renders it hot, never silently calm), which is why this is Minor. One-line tightening: skip only when `Sel.Name == "Code"`.

**MIN-3 — the count disclosure is incomplete.** `b9595b9`'s message says "board Go: 71/71 (base 66 + 5)"; truth is **70 (base 66 + 4)** — self-disclosed per the brief, and I confirm 70 is correct. But `9e5261f`'s message also states "board Go: 70/70" at a commit whose actual count is **69**; the parenthetical does flag that the fourth test lands "in the next commit," so it is a forward-looking end-state figure, but it is not true at its own SHA and was not part of the disclosure. Under the verification-honesty rule (suite / count / SHA / observer), a count is a claim about the SHA it names. History cannot be rewritten and should not be — the remedy is exactly this verdict carrying the corrected figures.

**MIN-4 — an ambiguous count in the §12b amendment.** `docs/design/2026-07-21-v0-yard-skeleton-design.md:485-487`: "observed live in the real store, `outcome: completed` x1, `outcome: approve-for-merge` x1 as of this car's base." Under the board-condition reading this is **true** (I confirmed the fold dedups by detail — `board/fold/algorithm.go:113,119` `if !contains(discoveries, d)` — so each distinct value yields exactly one condition). Under the natural "records in the store" reading it is **false by 4x and 5x** and contradicts `schema/vocab/outcomes.json:2`'s own `$comment` ("4 and 5 returned-*.json records respectively"), landed by the same car in the same train. A design doc readable two ways will be read both ways.

## THE COMPUTED-STYLE GATE (Playwright, real Chromium, real Go server, real store)

I built the server from this worktree via `board/web/test/support/real-board-server.js` (the house pattern), on an ephemeral port (4772/4773 — 4600 left alone), and drove it with `playwright@1.61.1` Chromium at 1280x900. Probe file was created at `board/web/_review_probe.mjs`, run, and **deleted**; `git status --porcelain` is empty.

**Run A — the REAL `artifacts/` store, untouched:**
```
board conditions on the wire: []
COLLAPSED STATE: { "stripPresent": false, ... }
lanes: Dispatches top=71 … (viewportH 900)
```
→ **(d) CONFIRMED end to end.** Both old discoveries are gone from the live board — not suppressed, but *no longer true*, because `schema/vocab/outcomes.json` now declares the words. The strip element is not rendered at all (`dom-writer.js:67` guard). Quiet-by-declaration, observed.

**Run B — a COPY of the store in `%TEMP%` (never the real store) with three injected records:** two valid `returned` records carrying undeclared outcomes, one carrying an undeclared field. Wire payload:
```
{"code":"record-unrecognised-fields", …, "register":"needs-attention"}
{"code":"discovery","detail":"outcome: totally-made-up-outcome","register":"nominal"}
{"code":"discovery","detail":"outcome: another-made-up-outcome","register":"nominal"}
```
Computed style, collapsed:
```
stripTag: "DETAILS", stripOpenAttr: false, stripOpenProp: false
summaryText: "1 FLAG + 2 notes"
summaryColor: "rgb(255, 74, 58)"        &lt;- --needs-attention #ff4a3a
groupsVisibleCollapsed: false
lanes: Dispatches top=109  (viewportH 900)
```
Computed style, expanded:
```
group "record-unrecognised-fields (x1)"  color rgb(255, 74, 58)
group "discovery (x2)"                   color rgb(217, 213, 201)   &lt;- --nominal #d9d5c9
instances: each carries its OWN register-* class and its own resolved color
```

- **(a) PASS** — native `&lt;details&gt;`, no `open` attribute, groups list `checkVisibility() === false`; the first yard lane sits at y=109 in a 900px viewport, well above the fold. Screenshot confirms: a single red `▶ 1 FLAG + 2 notes` line, then `DISPATCHES`.
- **(b) PASS** — the collapsed summary line's **computed** color is `rgb(255,74,58)`, the `--needs-attention` token, not a class-name assertion. Most-severe-wins across a FLAG group and a NOTE group, matching `REGISTER_ORDER` (`compose.js:13`, `rank` at `:15-20`).
- **(c) PASS** — expanding shows one row per code with `(xN)` counts and every per-instance detail verbatim, nothing discarded.
- **The finding a DOM test could never have produced:** the NOTE group renders `rgb(217,213,201)` **inside** a `register-needs-attention` strip. `.board-condition-instance` (`board.css`) deliberately declares no color and relies on its own `register-*` class — the #31 cascade-inheritance defect does **not** recur. This is exactly the #40 doctrine case, and the browser is the only witness.

Screenshots: `…\scratchpad\runB-injected-collapsed.png`, `…\scratchpad\runB-injected-expanded.png`.

## Adjudications the brief asked for

**1. The disclosed judgment call — `record-unrecognised-fields` at FLAG-tier: CONFIRMED, the car is right.**
I opened both cited rows and the citations are accurate at HEAD: `:333` (unknown fields) and `:335` (discovery). The discovery row carries "a discovery, not a bug"; the unknown-fields row carries no such framing — the car's stated reasoning holds. Two further pieces the car did not cite make it stronger: (i) `board/fold/algorithm.go:110-122` shows there is **no declaration mechanism** for record fields the way `outcomes.json` is one for outcomes — silencing an unrecognised field requires a *code* change to the typed decode (D17, design `:131`), so the #26-style quiet path the brief hypothesised does not exist yet, and NOTE-tier would leave a producer/reader contract drift permanently calm behind collapsed chrome (Law 4, silent loss); (ii) **empirically it does not cry wolf** — my Run A against the real 167-record store produced `board: []`, so FLAG-tier costs nothing today. The severity-philosophy counter-argument ("expected patterns are NOTES") would bite only once a declaration surface exists; when #26-style declaration lands for field names, this entry should be revisited, and `condition_severity.go:52-58` already says exactly that in the file where the next reader will find it. Good judgment, honestly disclosed, correctly conservative.

**2. The disclosed deviation — no wire-schema change: UPHELD.**
Verified independently, not accepted: `schema/yard-snapshot.schema.json` appears in none of the three commits' file lists. `$defs.boardCondition` already carried `code` (the grouping key) and `register` constrained to the three-value enum including `nominal`, so a discovery flipping calm stays schema-valid with no edit. Grouping is a total function of data already on the wire, and no instance detail is discarded (`render.js` `instances[]`). Observed end to end: a nominal-register discovery crossed the browser-side ingest gate and grouped correctly. The car's *rationale* leans on the inherited Rule-4 gloss (N-2), but the deviation is right on the merits under the design's actual text too — nothing in Rule 4 as written forbids view-side rollup. Nothing needed the schema.

**3. The unscoped enhancement — `overallBoardConditionsRegister`: KEEP, scope discipline satisfied.**
Not truly unscoped: the owner's design comment names "the register three-color law binds rendering" and HAVAGLANCE as binding laws, and HAVAGLANCE is *total transmission in a single look*. A collapsed strip whose line carries no severity signal hides a FLAG behind a click — that fails the named acceptance test. So this is the law applied, not scope creep; it is disclosed, ~4 lines, reuses `compose.js`'s existing combinator rather than a second copy, and carries its own test (TAP `ok 54`). **Correctness confirmed against the pinned taxonomy:** `mostSevereRegister` ranks by `REGISTER_ORDER = ['nominal','in-progress','needs-attention']` (`compose.js:13`), which TAP `ok 41` pins set-equal to `schema/yard-snapshot.schema.json`'s `$defs.register.enum`; unknown strings rank *most severe* (`compose.js:15-20`), never calm. Observed: one FLAG group + one NOTE group → collapsed line `rgb(255,74,58)`. Most-severe-wins, matching the pinned order.

## Round-2 findings

### MIN-A — `vocab.js:31-37` is stale at its own commit (two sites)

`docs/design/2026-07-21-v0-yard-skeleton-design.md:499-500` and `docs/design/2026-07-23-ui-mockup-brief.md:93` both cite `board/web/js/vocab.js:31-37` for `describeVocab`. Verified positions:

- at `8658011`: `describeVocab` occupied **31-37** exactly (31 = `export function describeVocab`, 37 = closing `}`)
- at `fdfa80f`: it occupies **39-45**, because **this same commit** added 8 comment lines to `vocab.js`'s header

The citation was written against the pre-fix file and invalidated by the very commit that wrote it — false at the one moment the North Star requires a document to be true. It lands on the JSDoc block, which does state *"unrecognised ids render their raw id as the label (verbatim) with register `needs-attention`"*, so **no reader is misled about substance** — that is why this is Minor and not Major. But it is the exact class this repo already scarred on (`board-defs.json`'s C2R-1: *"a line-number citation here rotted the instant a later commit in this same train inserted `$defs` above it"*), and that scar's own adopted remedy — cite by symbol, not line number — applies verbatim here.

### MIN-B — §6's row 339 left unmarked while Rule 4 got a marker

`design:339` still reads: `Unrecognised kind/outcome/position/role … register needs-attention - a discovery, not a bug`, with no supersession marker. §12b says it supersedes this row "IN PART", and the fix commit applied an inline `[SUPERSEDED …]` marker to Rule 4 at `:184` — but not here, 155 lines earlier in the same file.

Testing the premise rather than assuming asymmetry is a defect: this row is **half true** (`position`/`role` are genuinely unchanged), and §12b names and correctly scopes it, so the document is internally navigable. That distinguishes it from round-1's MAJOR-1, where Rule 4 and the gating matrix stated a false claim with *no* pointer anywhere. But §6 is the failure-modes table a reader actually consults, and for `kind`/`outcome` it states a register the code no longer produces. The fix demonstrated it knows the remedy by applying it upstream; one line closes it. **Minor — fix it for consistency, not a merge blocker.**

*Neither Minor was in my round-1 finding set, so neither is a fix that failed; both are new surfaces the fix opened or left uneven.*

## MAJOR-1 — The gate is inert-and-red under CI's shallow checkout, and CLAUDE.md now asserts the opposite

`CLAUDE.md:1034-1036`: *"It runs wherever `scripts/tests` runs - `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s 'Run board tooling tests' step already invokes `Invoke-Pester -Path ./scripts/tests`, so **no CI wiring change was needed**."* Same claim in the commit message.

`.github/workflows/ci.yml:119` is a bare `- uses: actions/checkout@v4` with **no `fetch-depth`** — default depth 1. I grepped the whole workflow: `fetch-depth|unshallow|--deepen` → **NONE FOUND**. The Entire-branch fetch at `:121-144` fetches one unrelated ref and does not unshallow. Checkout `:119` and the test step `:219` are in the same single job `verify` (`:112`).

The gate's derivation (`CodeCitationPolicy.Tests.ps1:75`) is `git diff --diff-filter=A &lt;BoundarySha&gt; HEAD`, and the boundary commit is not in a depth-1 clone. **Simulated it** (`git clone --depth 1`, 1 commit, HEAD `79cb5c9`):

```
fatal: bad object d4db6f5baf2bd31bf41f9dc5804684334797cb35
SHALLOW-CLONE (CI simulation) =&gt; Total=7 Passed=6 Failed=1
FAILED: ...finds added post-boundary code files to check (a check that examines nothing is not a pass)
  MESSAGE: Expected the actual value to be greater than 0, but got 0.
```

`ci.yml:227` exits 1 on `FailedCount -gt 0`. **CI goes red on the first push, for a reason unrelated to any citation violation** — the wolf-cry class CLAUDE.md names explicitly. `79cb5c9` is on **no remote branch** (`git branch -r --contains` empty), so this was never observed; the claim was derived by reading `ci.yml`, which is precisely the *reading is not verifying* scar. This is a Law 1 defect on doctrine text: CLAUDE.md is the highest-inheritance document in the repo and now carries a false mechanism claim.

**Credit where due:** the non-vacuity guard at `:99-101` is what converts this into a loud failure. Without it the empty added-set would have made the citation test pass vacuously — green, asserting nothing. Good design, and it caught its own author's untested claim.

**Remedy:** `fetch-depth: 0` (or enough to reach `d4db6f5`) on `ci.yml:119`, *plus* a pinned assertion that the boundary object is resolvable so the failure names the real cause; then correct `CLAUDE.md:1034-1036`.
