- `docs/templates/design-briefs.md` (**`Status: Current`**, `:3`) — `:105-124` is a section titled literally **"## 2. The DELTA re-review (after a fix, to the SAME reviewer)"**, the single most on-point home in the repo for a base-delta field. Its `VERDICT:` line at `:123` now omits a field the standing template calls mandatory. `:18-103` is "The design reviewer brief" with its own `VERDICT:` at `:84`.
- `docs/templates/worked-adversary-and-gate-briefs.md` — four reviewer briefs with four verdict shapes: design adversary (`:37`), spec adversary (`:64`), **plan adversary (`:74-91`)**, whole-branch gate (`:122`).
- `docs/templates/worked-briefs.md:86-132` — "## 2. The adversarial reviewer brief", `VERDICT:` at `:129`.

Two things make this Major rather than a tidy-up:

1. **The stated search could not have found the counter-example.** The glob `worked-*.md` structurally cannot match `design-briefs.md`. The one file carrying a dedicated DELTA re-review template is the one file the search excluded by construction.
2. **The obligation did not reach the rung that produced it.** The drill this ticket is built on was a **plan review** (`artifacts/reviews/2026-07-22-car2-plan-review-round2-drill.md`, Car 2's *plan*, round 2). The plan adversary brief is `worked-adversary-and-gate-briefs.md:74-91` and received nothing. This is the carrier rule's own failure mode — *obligations cross rungs by carrier, never by memory* — committed in the diff whose purpose is to install a carrier.

Per `.claude/agents/car.md:53-54`: "A diff that invalidates a document and leaves it stale is a MAJOR finding."

**Remedy:** either land the two fields in `design-briefs.md:105-124` and `worked-adversary-and-gate-briefs.md` §§1-4 in the same commit, or state in `car-brief.md` that the addendum is the **car-rung** brief and add an explicit cross-reference obliging the other rungs. Correct the commit-message claim on the way through.

### M-2 (#9, `71d0c25`) — `worked-spec.md:93` is a false contracts-touched row that contradicts its own document

New §9 row 1:

&gt; `` `docs/contracts/state-ledger.md` `` | Instantiates the **new mutable field** `Snapshot.verdict` with the old -&gt; delta -&gt; new arithmetic **from section 5** | Car B

Opening section 5 (which the row cites):

- `worked-spec.md:60` — `| Snapshot.verdict | **NOT STATE** - recomputed per snapshot | ... |`
- `worked-spec.md:63` — "Every new MUTABLE field (**none in this feature** - say so explicitly when true)"
- Section 5 contains **no arithmetic of any kind**.

So the row asserts a new mutable field where the cited section declares there is none, and cites arithmetic that section does not contain. `docs/contracts/state-ledger.md:11-14` confirms the ledger's subject is mutable process state with old→delta→new arithmetic, so the two readings cannot both stand.

The provenance makes it sharper: the real spec's corresponding row says the **opposite**, and correctly — `docs/specs/2026-07-22-dispatch-harness-spec.md:328`: *"Instantiated - records that the store is append-only under git and **process state is nil**."* The adaptation inverted the row's meaning while carrying its shape.

This is the exact failure the new §10 warns about three paragraphs later (`worked-spec.md:128-135`: *"a row claiming a section carried something that section did not actually say"*), shipped inside the exemplar that teaches the warning. A stranger copying §9 (Law 7 — and `docs/setup.md:43` names this file as *the* spec-rung exemplar) copies a contracts row that contradicts its own lifecycle section.

**Remedy:** make the row say what the fiction supports — e.g. "Instantiated - records that this feature adds no mutable process state (section 5), so the ledger's arithmetic is 0 -&gt; 0" — which also demonstrates the non-vacuity discipline `:87-89` claims for the section.

### M-3 (#9, `71d0c25`) — `worked-spec.md:119` is a false fidelity-ledger row; the cited sections are silent

New §10 row 7:

&gt; Owner decision: thresholds configurable per deployment, defaulted 30s/120s | **Section 2 (opening line), section 3**

I opened both, and grepped the whole file for every threshold token (`hreshold|configurab|30s|120s|deployment`). Hits: `:23`, `:41`, `:60`, `:70`, `:114`, `:119`. Therefore:

- **Section 2's opening line** (`:34-36`) — "derived in the adapter layer at snapshot arrival… carried ON the snapshot record". No configurability, no defaults. The only §2 threshold text is bullet 2 (`:41`), which is about *read-once* and says nothing about configurability or 30s/120s.
- **Section 3** (`:46-48`) — "closed string union; `Snapshot.verdict` required…; the banner consumes ONLY `verdict` + `fetchedAt`". **Zero** threshold references. The cited section is silent.

The claim's only actual home is `:23`, the document **header**, which is not a numbered section — which is itself the honest finding this table exists to surface (the owner decision was never carried into any section). Row 5 (`:117`) is soft in the same way: §2's opening line says "derived in the adapter layer", never "never the browser clock alone".

§10's own rule, one line above the table's failure (`:131-132`): *"it is checked by OPENING the cited section, never by trusting the row."* The exemplar demonstrates the false row rather than the discipline, and `:125-126` names the consequence itself: *"the fold that LOOKS folded."*

**Remedy:** either point row 7 at section 5's config-reload cell (`:60`, the only section text that acknowledges changing thresholds) plus a blank-is-a-defect note, or — better teaching — leave the row and mark it `NOT CARRIED - header only`, demonstrating what a fidelity ledger is *for*.

## MINOR FINDINGS

- **m-1 (#40, `car-brief.md:126-134`)** — the floor names *which* things to measure but never requires **reporting the observed values**. Issue #40's own carrier item 1 ends *"Report observed values."*, and the sibling line at `:112` ends *"Report observed."* A reviewer could name registers and states and assert correctness without printing a single `rgb()`. One clause fixes it.
- **m-2 (#13, `car-brief.md:168-169` vs `:94-95`)** — the VERDICT line requires "BASE-DELTA (#13) and **any** UNREPRODUCIBLE-EVIDENCE callouts… stated explicitly"; "any" qualifies the callouts, not BASE-DELTA, so the VERDICT line reads unconditional while `:94-95` reads conditional. Readable two ways, and I demonstrated it: as a round-1 reviewer I had to adjudicate my own template. Remedy: "BASE-DELTA (#13) **when this is a re-review**".
- **m-3 (#40, `.claude/agents/car.md:68-70`)** — the same commit amended GUARD CHECK in `car-brief.md:147-150` with the visual-guard clause and left the standing statement of that same rule in the reviewer agent definition unamended. `CLAUDE.md` explicitly names these two files as the paired carriers for brief mandates ("`docs/templates/car-brief.md`, `.claude/agents/car.md`, so it binds by template, not by memory").
- **m-4 (#9, `worked-spec.md:98`)** — "(design section 8, nine rows, with owners)". The named referent, the design-rung **template** `docs/templates/design-doc.md:123-126`, contains no table and **no rows**; the real design's §8 (`docs/design/2026-07-22-dispatch-harness-design.md:250-259`) has **eight** rows. Inherited verbatim from the round-1 verdict at `:67`, so the number's origin is the verdict — but a template is read cold and the parenthetical is uncheckable at either referent.
- **m-5 (#9, `worked-spec.md:159`)** — the heading reads "**(#9, closed 2026-07-26)**" while #9 is OPEN with two of its three closing requirements unmet, as the same block states at `:176-185`. The prior heading's parallel form ("KNOWN GAP - disclosed, not yet fixed (2026-07-22)") pushes the reader toward the wrong reading on the most-glanced line.
- **m-6 (#13, `car-brief.md:163-166`)** — the quotation **reorders** its source. Rendered as one quote with ellipses: *"a 'findings on unreproducible evidence' flag would have pointed me straight at C2R2-M1... M3/M4 were unverifiable-by-construction (gitignored probe)... it lives buried in a table cell"*. The source (`drill:110`) runs in the opposite order: M3/M4 clause → "yet it lives buried in a table cell" → "A 'findings on unreproducible evidence' flag would have pointed me straight at C2R2-M1." Every substring is verbatim; the sequence is not, and ellipses elide rather than permute. In a repo whose verdicts are sha256-covered and land verbatim, a permuted quote in a standing template is a real defect. (Quote 1, `car-brief.md:100-104` against `drill:103`, is **verbatim and correctly cited** — I checked it word for word.)
- **m-7 (#13, `car-brief.md:99-100`)** — "could reconstruct **every prior finding** from the landed verdict except one thing" overstates the drill. `drill:105`: *"The only inheritable blind spot (M3/M4's gitignored probe) was recorded honestly as UNVERIFIABLE"* — M3/M4 were **not** reconstructable. That is the second thing, and it is the entire basis for the second addition in the same commit.

## THE SENTENCE CHECK (documentation form)

**#9's sentence:** verdict `2026-07-22-harness-spec-round1-REJECT.md:67-68` → ticket #9 body ("What closing this requires", 3 items) → `worked-spec.md:85-135` (the fold) → `docs/setup.md:43` ("Use it directly", naming this file as *the* spec-rung exemplar) → a future spec author with zero context. **The sentence breaks at the last hop:** that author copies §9 row 1 and §10 row 7, and inherits two rows that are false against the very sections they cite. The exemplar transmits the shape correctly and the discipline incorrectly.

**#13's sentence:** `drill:109-110` (two template GAPs) → ticket #13 → `car-brief.md:94-104`, `:158-166`, `:168-169` → a dispatched reviewer brief → the landed verdict record. **The branch that runs through the design, spec, plan and gate rungs never receives the field** (M-1) — including the plan rung, where the drill that wrote the finding actually ran.

**#40's sentence:** #31 (live defect, 50 tests green) → #33 (adopt a real browser) → the Car 31 reviewer's hand-built cascade model → #40's carrier proposal (3 items) → `car-brief.md:126-150` → the view-30 round-1 verdict `:137-176` as the worked proof. **This one traces end to end.** All three of #40's proposed items landed; item 2's judgment half is preserved verbatim from the ticket ("a spelling check with better instrumentation"), which was the ticket's central worry.

**Cold-reader test (#40c):** the trigger is workable — the CHECK fires on touching a rendered surface, and the EVIDENCE BAR binds claims about rendering, so a comment-only JS diff carries the line and finds no rendering claim to settle. "anything a user SEES" is a broad catch-all but errs toward inclusion, which is the right direction for a floor. Two soft edges: no explicit demand to report values (m-1), and "collapsed/expanded states" is specific to the board-conditions strip and reads oddly for a non-collapsible surface. Neither is disqualifying.

## #9 DISPOSITION — my recommendation

**Do not close #9 now** — M-2 and M-3 are defects in the very sections item 1 delivers, so item 1 is not yet done. After they are fixed:

**Close #9, having first split items 2 and 3 into their own issues, cross-linked.** Reasoning:

1. **The ticket's own deadline is the fold.** #9 states the debt "must close **before the next spec is written from `docs/templates/worked-spec.md`**". That urgency attaches entirely to item 1. Once §9/§10 are correct, the reason #9 was time-boxed is discharged, and leaving it open makes the board show a docs gap that no longer exists — Law 1 pointed at this project's own status surface, which is its whole thesis.
2. **Item 2 is a different KIND of work under a different label.** "Make CI walk the ledger" is a mechanism-tier gate: code, a CI step, red-first proof. Its assertion is already specified (spec round 4: *the annotation must be a substring of the target*) and its pattern already exists in-repo (`docs/templates/repo-policy-check-patterns.md` §1, landed as `scripts/tests/DocPolicy.Tests.ps1`). It belongs in a tooling/repo-policy train where it can be sized and dispatched, not carried as a tail on a docs ticket — and N-2 above is free evidence for whoever picks it up that the exemplar's own DR-1 row will fail that check.
3. **Item 3 can never be verified from here, and that is precisely why it needs its own ticket.** A closure condition unverifiable in the repo the issue lives in makes for a permanently-open umbrella. It is also newly urgent in one direction I can name without leaving this repo: as of `71d0c25` the upstream note that item 3's disclosure describes no longer exists here, so whatever `Project-Bootstrapping` ships now describes a hole this repo has filled. **I cannot confirm that** — different repository, not present in this worktree — and I flag it as unreproducible rather than assert it.

Closing a ticket whose named scope ("worked-spec.md is missing the two carrier sections") is complete, with two differently-shaped residuals tracked in their own right, is the honest bookkeeping. Keeping it open as a two-tailed umbrella is how a ticket becomes furniture.

## MAJOR-3 (new, round 2) — the closed-set guard silently swallows extensionless files

`CodeCitationPolicy.Tests.ps1:213-227`. I committed an uncited extensionless post-boundary file (`Dockerfile`): **10/10 green.** Traced the mechanism rather than inferring it:

```
Dockerfile present in added non-artifact set: True
observed extensions: '' '.go' '.js' '.json' '.md' '.mjs' '.ps1' '.sh'
unaccounted .Count = 1
unaccounted elements = ''
join result = ''
does the ASSERTION treat the join as empty? -&gt; True
```

The guard **detects it correctly** — `$unaccounted` holds one element — and then `:223`'s `$unaccounted -join ', ' | Should -BeNullOrEmpty` discards it, because the joined empty extension *is* an empty string. A computed truth is dropped one line before it reaches the surface (**Law 4**). Localized to `:223`: the same idiom at `:201` is safe, since a violator is a file path and never empty.

The disclosure runs the wrong way. `:56-58` claims "**every** extension actually observed in the post-boundary, non-artifacts corpus must be in checked-union-exempt, or the suite reds **BY NAME**," and `CLAUDE.md:~1030` repeats it. Measurably false — so **doctrine now carries the claim** (**Law 1**). Not exotic: `Dockerfile`, `Makefile`, `CODEOWNERS`, extensionless shell scripts.

**Remedy, one line:** assert `$unaccounted.Count | Should -Be 0` and render `''` as a sentinel such as `(no extension)` in the message — the detection already works.

*Why this is Major and not a nit: I rejected round 1 for this exact class — a completeness guard with a silent hole and an overstating disclosure. Applying a softer bar now, because the fix is otherwise good work, is the trained agreeableness this shop's guide star names. The class is identical; so is the ruling.*

## The disclosed process slip — no residue

The slip (a `git reset --hard` during the M2 red-first discarding in-progress uncommitted edits, caught and redone) put exactly the M1 and MIN-3 work at risk. Both landed complete and accurate, and I verified them independently rather than trusting the redo: `ci.yml:128-130` present with its full rationale comment and **proven in both directions**; `docs/setup.md:42` present and accurate. Cross-reference sweep found **no dead citations** — the header's `DocPolicy.Tests.ps1:26` claim is verbatim true ("*The closed set. Growing it is a deliberate decision, not a convenience.*"), the ci.yml claim is true, the fixture-README claim is true, the 22-file count reproduces. **Clean tree, no half-done pieces.** The slip cost time, not correctness.

**r1: 2 Major, 4 Minor, 1 Note → r2: 1 Major, 0 Minor.** Majors declining; all four Minors and the Note closed and verified closed.

Swirl trigger, honestly walked: **(a) counts declining — does NOT fire.** **(b) same-section clustering — partial**, both M2 and M3 sit in extension coverage, but r1's other Major is fully closed and did not recur. **(c) a finding created by the previous round's fix — FIRES**, MAJOR-3 is new-in-round-2.

**Ruling: converging, not swirling — I set NO cap.** The trigger's purpose is detecting a defect being *relocated* rather than resolved; here r1-M2 ("no completeness check exists") was genuinely resolved and *measured* resolved for the case that matters, leaving a narrower adjacent assertion-shape bug. That is progress, not station-keeping. **But I name (c) explicitly for the conductor:** this should be the last round. A round-3 finding again inside extension coverage *is* the pattern, and should escalate rather than iterate.

## MAJOR — the one blocker

### M-4 (NEW, revealed by `3e267e2`'s own fix) — the plan rung's delegated verdict-shape carrier still has neither field

`docs/templates/worked-adversary-and-gate-briefs.md:88` — the plan adversary's own heading:

&gt; `## 3. The plan adversary (rule 5 - **see worked-plan.md's review record for the verdict shape**)`

That pointer leads to `docs/templates/worked-plan.md:169`, titled **"The plan-review record (rule 5) - what the adversary hunts, and the verdict shape."** It was not touched by this delta, and it carries:

- **`:171-181` — the same dimension list, now diverged.** `worked-plan.md` states Dimensions **(a)-(e)**; `3e267e2` changed the same rung's brief to **(a)-(g)**. Two copies of one rung's dimension list, in disagreement, with no cross-reference marking either authoritative. The carrier rule sanctions restatement *across* rungs ("four restatements of one fact, and the redundancy IS the point") — this is duplication *within* one rung, which it does not.
- **`:183-189` — the verdict shape itself**, the thing `:88` sends readers to, with no BASE-DELTA and no unreproducible-evidence line.
- **`:191-192`** — "the re-review verifies the delta; the verdict history stays in the plan" — literally the delta-re-review path, at the plan rung, which is the rung where the drill that produced #13 ran.

**The traceable path to a non-compliant artifact:** a conductor writes a plan re-review brief from `worked-adversary-and-gate-briefs.md` §3, follows `:88` to `worked-plan.md` for the verdict shape, writes the verdict in the shape found there, and produces a verdict with no BASE-DELTA — having followed both templates correctly. That is the same shape as M-1, one pointer further out.

I am classifying this as a **new finding rather than M-1 DRIFTED**, because M-1's named remedy was delivered completely and correctly in all five carriers I named. This is a sixth carrier reachable only through a delegation pointer.

**And I must disclose my own round-1 miss:** my round-1 search was `VERDICT:|verdict template|reviewer brief|Reviewer brief`. `worked-plan.md` says *"the verdict shape"* and *"The plan-review record"* and matched none of them. I faulted the car for a glob that structurally could not see a file, using a pattern that structurally could not see a file — same class, one directory over. The car's fix was correct against the finding it was given; the finding was incomplete.

**Remedy (small):** add both fields to `worked-plan.md:169-195` in that section's own narrative voice, extend its dimension list to (f)/(g) to match, and add a one-line cross-reference in one direction so the plan rung's dimension list has a single owner.

## MINOR

- **m-8 — `design-briefs.md:137-139` misstates the drill, and inverts its verdict on a prior reviewer.** The WHY reads: *"a finding built on a gitignored probe log **was recorded as closed with no flag that the closure itself was unverifiable by anyone else**."* Both halves are false against the cited source. `drill:66` records C2R1-M4 as **"DRIFTED → C2R2-M1"** — not closed; promoted to that round's own Major. `drill:105` says the blind spot *"**was recorded honestly as UNVERIFIABLE, not papered over** — a fresh reviewer inherits the correct blindness."* The drill's actual complaint (`:110`) was **placement** — "it lives buried in a table cell" — never absence of disclosure. This turns a landed verdict's explicit praise of a prior reviewer's honesty into an accusation that they papered something over, in a public repo. **The parallel WHY the same commit wrote at `worked-adversary-and-gate-briefs.md:119-120` gets it exactly right** ("no *standing flag* that it was unreproducible") — two adjacent adaptations, one accurate, one not. Graded Minor for consistency with how I graded round-1's m-7 (same class: inaccurate provenance gloss, correct instruction above it) — but it is the sharpest Minor in the set.
- **m-9 — `3e267e2`'s message mis-describes its own diff.** It claims "**lettered** ATTACK SURFACE items for the design adversary". They are unlettered standalone blocks placed after `(h)` (`:37-42`). The plan adversary's `(f)`/`(g)` and the gate's `9`/`10` claims are both true. **The shape choice is correct** — BASE-DELTA is a reporting obligation, not an attack surface, so keeping it outside the lettered list is right; only the description is wrong. Remedy is a record-correction line in a later commit message, not a code change.
- **m-10 — the plan adversary's `(f)`/`(g)` fit the structure but not the voice.** `(a)`-`(e)` are one terse clause each; `(f)` runs six lines (`:100-105`) and `(g)` four (`:105-109`), and `(f)` carries justification prose ("this field is what lets a rotation reviewer confirm the delta from the verdict alone…") that the `[WHY]` block added immediately below at `:115-121` already states. Both files' convention is explicit that reasoning lives in bracketed WHY blocks and gets deleted when copying. A conductor copying §3 into a real brief gets a ten-line `(f)` reading unlike its neighbours. **This is my ruling on the question the brief asked:** structure correct, voice not. Remedy: compress `(f)`/`(g)` to one clause each, let the existing WHY carry the reasoning.

My own grep confirms both rows' claims are true.

**Cold-reader re-test — no new ambiguity introduced.** The m-2 conditional now reads cleanly. The NOT CARRIED rows are self-contained: each names its own grep evidence, names the header as the only home, and names the nearest near-miss. A reader needs neither my verdict nor the commit message.

Round 1: **3 Major / 7 Minor / 4 Notes.** Round 2: **1 Major / 3 Minor / 2 Notes.** All 11 round-1 findings closed with verifiable fixes; three were fixed *beyond* the finding (row 5, the contiguous requote, the class-level re-read).

I am **setting a cap rather than escalating**, because the clustering is structural to the ticket — #13 *is* a placement question, so every finding about it lands in one area by construction — and the fix quality is high. But both Majors in this series came from **a search that could not see a file**, and a third recurrence would mean the class was never addressed, only its instances.

**THE CAP — what round 3 must demonstrate:** a **mechanically derived, complete inventory** of every reviewer-brief or verdict-shape carrier in the repo (not a pattern-guess — enumerate `docs/templates/*.md` and `.claude/agents/*.md` in full and open each), presented as a table with each file marked *has both fields* / *not applicable, and why*. Fixing `worked-plan.md` alone does not clear this cap. **Failing to produce that inventory escalates to the owner rather than to round 4.**

Disclosed edge: a genuinely comment-incapable extensionless file would need a future *path*-level exemption, never an extension-level one (an extension-level `''` exemption would silently swallow every future Dockerfile too — the same defect one layer up). Extracted `Get-UnaccountedExtensions` and added a new `Describe 'Closed-set completeness mechanism (synthetic...)'` with 3 unit tests proving the fix in isolation, since this repo's real corpus has no unaccounted-extensionless case to naturally exercise that branch.

**Self-found and fixed in the same commit** (not a named finding, disclosed): while building the synthetic proof, discovered that PowerShell silently collapses a function's returned array to a scalar (or `$null`) at the *call site* when exactly 0 or 1 items are returned, regardless of `return @(...)` inside the function — probed directly (`function F { return @('') }; $r = F` leaves `$r.GetType()` as `string`). Fixed every call site of `Get-AddedCodeFiles`, `Get-AddedNonArtifactFiles`, `Get-UnaccountedExtensions` by wrapping with `@(...)` at the assignment.

|---|---|---|

| Cited extensionless `Dockerfile` | — | **13/13 green** (no false positive) |

## The policy decision — ADJUDICATED: correct rule, accurately described

**Extensionless files belong in the CHECKED set, and the car's reasoning is right.** The distinction it draws is the load-bearing one: `.json`/`.md` are exempt for *format* reasons (JSON cannot hold a comment without mutating the artifact under test) or *ownership* reasons (DocPolicy owns docs). Extensionless files share neither property — the class is defined by naming convention, not format, and its real members (`Dockerfile`, `Makefile`, `CODEOWNERS`, shebang scripts) are `#`-comment-capable, exactly as much as a `.sh`.

**The failure-direction argument is the decisive one and the car got it right.** Checking by default means a genuinely comment-incapable extensionless file produces a *loud named violator*. Exempting `''` would produce a *silent pass for every Dockerfile forever* — reproducing this very defect one layer up. So **path-level, never extension-level** is correct: a path exemption is bounded to one artifact; an extension exemption is unbounded over all future files.

**Accurate to what the code does?** Verified: `$CodeExtensions` includes `''` at `:205`; my uncited-Dockerfile injection reds. The disclosed edge does **not** claim a path-exemption mechanism exists — it says one "would need" to be built "if one ever appears ... None exists today; calibrate then." That is honest and NIRTS-correct, not an overstatement.

**Probe claim verified:** no post-boundary extensionless file exists today. I derived the observed set independently: `.go .js .json .md .mjs .ps1 .sh` — all covered.

## The mechanism fix and the self-found fix — both verified

- **Assertion on `.Count` with sentinel** (`:277-282`): correct, and the sentinel is proven by fault injection above.
- **`Get-UnaccountedExtensions` extracted, and the synthetic tests call the PRODUCTION function** — my MIN-1 class is honored. Grep confirms **one definition each** of all five helpers, and all **7 call sites** wrapped with `@()`.
- **Array-collapse claim — PROBED TRUE:** `function F1 { return @('') }; $r = F1` yields **`String`, `IsArray = False`**; a zero-item return yields **`NULL`**. The `@()` wrapping is genuinely load-bearing, not decoration. The car's stated probe matches mine exactly.
- **`Mandatory` removal — PROBED TRUE:** `[Parameter(Mandatory)][string[]]` rejects an empty-string element verbatim — *"Cannot bind argument to parameter 'X' because it is an empty string."* Removing it was **necessary**, not a loosening of convenience, and it is safe: both `Get-AddedCodeFiles` call sites (`:227-228`, `:364-365`) still pass `-CodeExtensions`, and an omission would red the non-vacuity test loudly rather than pass silently.

My r2 finding was that doctrine asserted completeness the guard did not deliver. It now states exactly what the guard does, records the round-3 correction with my own measurement quoted accurately ("an uncited extensionless Dockerfile passed 10/10 green"), and says outright that the sentence "and the test header were both corrected to state exactly what the guard now delivers rather than restating the round-2 overclaim." **I re-tested every claim in the new text against the code; no overstatement remains.**

A post-boundary `.gitignore`/`.dockerignore`/`.editorconfig` will **red the closed-set test naming it**. ### Ticket #27 — commit `3fd4fa6`

**Design choice (disclosed):** wire keeps `elapsed_seconds` exact; only the change-detection comparison quantises it. Chosen because `board/web/js/dom-writer.js:201` renders `elapsed_seconds` verbatim to the second (`${d.elapsedSeconds}s`) — no schema change, no consumer impact. New `elapsedSecondsBucketGranularity = 60` (seconds, `board/server/poll.go`) buckets the comparison copy only, via new `bucketDispatchesForComparison` inside `mustMarshalStripped`. A `dispatched`→`overdue` transition still bumps `seq` regardless of the bucket since that changes the `state` string, untouched by the quantisation.

**Red:** `TestPollOnceElapsedSecondsBucketedForChangeDetection` (`board/server/elapsedbucket_test.go`) failed with: `elapsed_seconds moving 10-&gt;50 (same 60s bucket) must NOT bump seq; seq stayed 1 then moved to 2` — reproducing the churn exactly.
**Green:** same test passes after the fix; also asserts a bucket-crossing (50s→65s) still bumps seq, and that a mid-bucket poll serves the *prior* snapshot's exact value (matches `PollOnce`'s pre-existing "prior snapshot stands unchanged" contract, same as `ageBucketMs` already relies on).

**Docs same-commit:** `docs/contracts/gating-matrix.md`'s disclosed-finding paragraph marked RESOLVED with evidence; `board/web/js/ingest.js`'s seq-ordering comment trued up (no longer describes #27 as live churn).

## 2. #27 semantics — mechanism re-derived, four transitions observed

PROBE A poll1: seq=1 state="dispatched" elapsed=10 freshness="fresh"
PROBE A poll2: seq=2 state="overdue"    elapsed=25 changed=true
PROBE A poll3: seq=2 changed=false (want no bump)
PROBE B poll1: seq=1 changed=true
PROBE B poll2: seq=2 changed=true
```

| Transition the brief asked me to prove | Observed |
|---|---|
| Two polls, in-flight dispatch, inside one bucket → NO seq bump | ✓ car's `elapsedbucket_test.go:89-98` (10s→50s, `changed=false`); my PROBE A poll3 (25s→40s, overdue, `changed=false`) |
| Bucket crossing → bump | ✓ `elapsedbucket_test.go:106-118` (50→65, seq+1) |
| A record landing mid-bucket → bump | ✓ **PROBE B** (new subject at elapsed 30, same bucket 0, `changed=true`) |
| `dispatched`→`overdue` → bump **regardless of bucket** | ✓ **PROBE A poll2**: budget 20s, elapsed 10→25, both bucket 0, `changed=true`, `seq 1→2` |

The car's claim that the state string carries the overdue transition is **true and now proven, not asserted**. The path: `board/fold/algorithm.go:240` sets `entry.State = "overdue"` while `winnerKind` stays `"dispatched"` (`algorithm.go:204-208`), so `MarshalJSON` (`board/fold/output.go:24-26`) still emits `elapsed_seconds` for an overdue entry — bucketing keeps applying past the budget (PROBE A poll3 confirms), and `"state"` is untouched by `bucketDispatchesForComparison`.

**Red-first re-derived by fault injection.** Removed `poll.go:483-485`, ran the car's pin:

```
elapsedbucket_test.go:94: elapsed_seconds moving 10-&gt;50 (same 60s bucket) must NOT bump seq;
                          seq stayed 1 then moved to 2
--- FAIL: TestPollOnceElapsedSecondsBucketedForChangeDetection
```

Reverted; `sha256(board/server/poll.go) = 87d2f0b3d43dea4c566a2363f1d58ea66e7f86b70eb668d1be3dc696dd292cc8`, byte-identical to pre-injection.

### The mid-bucket wire corner — ruling: the trade is SOUND, the comment describing it is NOT

The brief asked me to rule on the displayed number lagging. **The lag is real and the car's own test pins it**: `elapsedbucket_test.go:99-101` asserts that at a poll where actual elapsed is **50s**, the served snapshot carries **10** — 40 seconds stale, because `PollOnce` returns the prior snapshot unchanged.

*Is the trade sound?* **Yes.** Nothing downstream computes on `elapsedSeconds`. The only consumer is `board/web/js/dom-writer.js:200-202`, which prints `` `${d.elapsedSeconds}s` ``; `render.js:188` passes it through with a type guard. There is no client countdown and no client-side overdue derivation — `overdue` is a server-side promotion at `algorithm.go:238-240`, and that transition bumps seq immediately (PROBE A). So the loss is confined to a display number refreshing in ~60s steps on a glance surface whose alarm-bearing field (`state`) is exact. For a Solari board that is the right call.

*Is the comment describing it sound?* **No — see M3.**

---

## 3. #29 semantics — taxonomy attacked, ticket fidelity checked

**Mechanism.** `computeLiveFreshness` (`poll.go:399`) gained the fold's dispatch entries, threaded from `buildSnapshot` at `poll.go:308`; `hasInFlightDispatch` (`poll.go:428-436`) returns true for `State == "dispatched" || State == "overdue"`. `poll.go:424-427`: in flight → `stale`; otherwise → `idle`, both carrying the same `ageBucketMs`.

**Red-first re-derived.** Collapsed `poll.go:424-427` to always return `stale`:

```
freshnessidle_test.go:50: freshness.kind = "stale", want idle - nothing is in flight
                          (the sole record already returned), so an old, quiet yard
                          must render calm, never stale-hot
--- FAIL: TestPollOnceIdleWhenNothingInFlight
```

Under the same injection `TestPollOnceStaleWhenInFlightAndDataStopsMoving` stays **PASS** — correctly a discriminator, not a red-first pin, which is exactly how the car describes it (`freshnessidle_test.go:53-59`). Reverted byte-identical (hash above).

### Attack 1: is overdue-in-flight rendering the lane merely "stale" the right call?

Probed it (PROBE C: `dispatched` with budget 20s, polled at elapsed 600s, default `stalenessMs`):

```
PROBE C: lane freshness kind="stale", entry state="overdue"
```

**Right call.** Both halves are hot: the lane's freshness resolves `stale` → `needs-attention` via `compose.js:39`, and the ROW resolves `overdue` → `needs-attention` from `schema/vocab/board-defs.json`'s `liveness` array. No severity is lost anywhere. There is no state in which an overdue dispatch makes any surface calmer than it was at base.

### Attack 2: does `idle` conflate OBSERVATION freshness with ACTIVITY recency?

Probed the two edges the brief named:

```
PROBE D: presumed-lost yard    -&gt; lane freshness kind="idle", entry state="presumed-lost"
PROBE E: ruling-only store (no dispatch subjects), 60min old -&gt; kind="idle", dispatch entries=0
```

- **Presumed-lost → idle lane.** Defensible, and the truth is not lost: `board-defs.json` maps `presumed-lost` → `needs-attention`, so the ROW renders hot inside a calm lane. That is Rule 1 operating as designed (lane register = most-severe of position × freshness × capability; row registers are their own axis — the same separation `#31`'s guard exists to protect). Not a finding.
- **No dispatch subjects at all → idle.** Matches the car's documented rule and the gating-matrix Idle row.

**The poller-dead question — ruling: the gap is REAL, PRE-EXISTING, and OUT OF SCOPE for this diff.** I traced it: `lastPollAt` exists (`poll.go:70`, set at `:235`, read at `:178`) but reaches **no** wire field — I grepped every non-test use. The SSE heartbeat is a **per-connection** ticker (`handlers.go:76`, fired at `:92`), independent of `RunPollLoop` (`poll.go:197-217`). So a wedged poll loop leaves a client "connected" and showing a frozen snapshot forever.

But that is not a loss #29 caused, and here is the disproof of the framing: **pre-#29 `stale` never carried poller-health information either.** Spec YB-15 exists precisely to prove staleness fires while the scan keeps succeeding on schedule (`TestDemoModeIsWireDisclosureOnlyAndStalenessStillFires`), and a wedged poller freezes whatever kind was last computed — `fresh` just as easily as `stale`. Zero information was removed. The ticket's third axis, scan-health, **is** implemented and predates this diff: `Scan` error → `failed` (`poll.go:275-281`, gating-matrix Failed-panel row). Recommend a ticket for a poll-loop-liveness surface; it is not this car's debt.

### Ticket fidelity to #29's own text

&gt; "distinguish scan-health (did the poll succeed), data-age, and YARD ACTIVITY (is anything in flight) — an idle yard should render calm/nominal with its age honestly shown, going stale-hot only when something IS in flight and the data stops moving, or the scan fails."

All three axes present and correctly wired; the calm-with-age-shown requirement is met literally (`idle, quiet 3040s`, observed in a browser). **High fidelity.** The ticket's second sentence — *"Also retune stalenessMs against measured reality"* — is untouched and undisclosed. See **m1**.

Run: this worktree's own Go binary, built on the fly, port 4931, **real `artifacts/` store, no `STARCAR_STORE_PATH` override**, real Chromium via the Playwright library.

**Hop 0 — the real-store premise, re-derived independently.** I did not take the car's scan on trust. A PowerShell pass over all 189 records: `dispatched=61, returned=122, intent=6`; **87 dispatch-kind subjects; 0 whose latest-`at` winner is `dispatched`.** The live server agreed exactly: `dispatch entry states: {"returned":87}, total 87`. The car's claim is **TRUE**.

## 5. THE #31 GUARD REWIRE — still guards, at the same strength, PROVEN

**Adjudication: the rewire is honest and the guard is undiminished.**

- **Same strength.** Still the real Go binary (`real-board-server.js:97` `go build ./server`), real static files, real Chromium, `getComputedStyle` — every one of the #33 amendment's constraints intact. What changed is only *which store path* the real binary reads, via the server's own `STARCAR_STORE_PATH` env var (`real-board-server.js:169`, consumed by `config.go`'s `applyEnvOverrides`) — **not a second store-selection mechanism**.
- **Honest fixture.** `real-board-server.js:64-80` writes a record carrying all seven schema-required fields (`schema`, `kind`, `subject`, `session_id`, `at`, `normalisation`, `integrity`). It is a real `dispatched` record shape, not a synthetic freshness stub, and it is one *addition* to a byte-identical copy of every committed record.
- **No risk to the real store.** `cpSync(join(REPO_ROOT,'artifacts'), storeDir, {recursive:true})` — source is `artifacts/`, destination is `mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'starcar-board-scratch-store-'))`. Direction correct; every write (`mkdirSync`, `writeFileSync`) targets `storeDir`. Verified after all my runs: `git status --porcelain` shows nothing under `artifacts/`.
- **NON-VACUITY RE-PROVEN BY ME, through a real browser (#40).** Deleted `color: var(--nominal);` from `.register-nominal` (`board/web/css/board.css:168`) and ran the guard:

```
✖ issue #31: a nominal-register dispatch row inside a needs-attention-registered lane ...
  AssertionError: ... computed color rgb(255, 74, 58), but board.css's own .register-nominal
  rule renders as rgb(217, 213, 201) when nothing overrides it.
  actual:   'rgb(255, 74, 58)'
  expected: 'rgb(217, 213, 201)'
```

This is computed-style evidence, not a DOM-class assertion. It also proves the **precondition** survived the rewire: execution reached line 125, so the assertion at `:111-117` (`laneIsNeedsAttention === true`) passed — the scratch store's seeded in-flight dispatch really does drive the lane to `register-needs-attention` under the #29 rules, and the `solari-row register-nominal unassigned` row really exists. Restored: `sha256(board/web/css/board.css) = 07c916278138370bdcef782c5ec0316b9972dfe6398ddd03611428aa1091a63d`, byte-identical; guard back to **2/2 pass**.

One stale sentence rides along: `browser-register-cascade.test.js:115` still says *"the real store's records should always read stale relative to 'now'"* inside the precondition message — the premise the rewire exists to remove. It is a failure-message string, factually superseded by the file's own header at `:28-42`. Folding it into **M2** as a third site is defensible; I have left it as an advisory line under M2 rather than inflating the count, since the header immediately above it states the correct premise.

## 6. RETRO-CONCERN RULING — the amendment path **SATISFIES** it; no retroactive design round

The concern, verified verbatim at `docs/retros/2026-07-23-board-train-retro.md:120-121`:

&gt; "the staleness/idle semantics (#29) touch the freshness union, which is CLOSED by mechanism - growing it is a design-rung decision, not a patch."

**Ruling: CLEARED for this change.** Reasoning, against the two sections the brief names:

1. **Right-sizing (structural vs coverage).** It touches a cross-boundary contract, which argues structural. But measure the actual shape: one *additive* `oneOf` branch whose field set is byte-identical to the branch beside it; one entry in a map that already existed to be keyed by kind (`compose.js:42-49`); one `case` in a `switch` that already had a hot default for unknown kinds (`compose.js:120-124`); one Go conditional. **No new register, no new field, no new mechanism, no new consumer path.** The register enum — the thing the constitution actually reserves as constitution-level — is untouched. That is coverage-class in everything but the file it lands in.
2. **Match the instrument to the artifact.** This is decisive and it cuts *against* the retro's default. CLAUDE.md: a format or wire contract *"goes straight to an EXECUTABLE spec: a schema file, a conformance test vector, a red-first test that fails for the stated reason. The artifact IS the precision."* That is precisely what landed — and I verified all three tiers myself: the schema branch, a red I re-derived by fault injection with the failure quoted verbatim, and computed-style evidence from a real browser. A prose design round would have produced *a photograph of this machine*. Ordering one here would be the ceremony-porting failure the right-sizing rule names as itself a process failure.
3. **Precedent.** #30 made a comparable closed-taxonomy change through car + §12b amendment + adversarial review; it landed and is visible in the same table (`gating-matrix.md:47`). Re-litigating the rung for an identical shape is inconsistency, not rigour.
4. **The car did not self-adjudicate.** It reproduced the concern verbatim in the durable artifact (`design ...§12b`, "Disclosed process note") and routed it upward. That is the escalation reflex working as written: *report a contradiction, never resolve one you do not own.* The car earns credit here, not a finding.

**Stated against myself, because the honest ruling has a cost line:** the lighter rung *did* miss something a design moment would likely have caught — issue #29's second explicit ask (`retune stalenessMs`) and its interaction with the shipped 15s default. That is real evidence for the retro's instinct. It is recorded as **m1** at Minor. It is a reason to sharpen the amendment, not to re-run the rung.

`3fd4fa6` inserted a 29-line comment block at `poll.go:27-55`, shifting everything below it, and in the same commit authored a new test file citing the *pre-shift* coordinates.

|---|---|---|---|
| `elapsedbucket_test.go:17` | `poll.go:399-412` | the `DISCLOSED, OUT OF SCOPE` #27 comment (verified via `git show 8b9df53:board/server/poll.go`) | `func computeLiveFreshness(...)` and its loop body — unrelated |
| `elapsedbucket_test.go:27` | `poll.go:190-196` | `PollOnce`'s doc comment, containing the exact quoted phrase *"otherwise the prior snapshot stands unchanged"* | `func (s *Server) EndPoll()` + `RunPollLoop`'s header |
| `elapsedbucket_test.go:83` | `poll.go:190-196` | same | same |

The quoted contract now lives at `poll.go:219-225` (phrase on `:223`). Worse than the precedent: those citations were born stale *one commit after* their invalidation; these were born stale *in* it.

**Precedent binding this severity:** `artifacts/reviews/2026-07-23-board-car2-review-round1.md:97,109` — identical class, ruled **MAJOR**, gated the merge, with the note that "severity tracks consequence to the doc-equal-to-code standard, not fix cost."

### **M2 (MAJOR) — two sibling comments falsified by these commits and left standing, one of them six lines from the change that falsified it**

**(a) `board/web/js/compose.js:91-92`** — inside the file `4f06a4c` edited:

&gt; "the wire's `fresh` variant carries no `ageBucketMs` at all - **only `stale` does** (`schema/yard-snapshot.schema.json` `$defs.freshness`'s `oneOf`)."

At HEAD that `oneOf` has **two** branches requiring `ageBucketMs`: `stale` (`schema:37-44`) and `idle` (`schema:45-53`) — added by this commit, and `case 'idle'` sits at `compose.js:102`, six lines below the false sentence.

**(b) `board/web/test/ingest.test.js:66-69`** — falsified by `3fd4fa6`:

&gt; "Issue #27: while a dispatch is actively running, `elapsed_seconds` is unquantised and can bump `seq` on nearly every poll"

False at HEAD. Disproven by the car's own `elapsedbucket_test.go:89-98` and by my PROBE A poll3 (`changed=false`). This is a lying canary teaching a future reader exactly the belief #27 was filed to correct. The car **fixed the twin** of this comment in `board/web/js/ingest.js:51-60` in that same commit and said so in the commit message — so the class was known and one instance was missed. That is the "fixed the code, skipped the record" shape the PR-cycle scar names.

*Advisory, same class, not counted separately:* `browser-register-cascade.test.js:115` still asserts "the real store's records should always read stale relative to 'now'" in a failure message, contradicting its own header at `:28-42`.

**Fix:** three sentences.

### **M3 (MAJOR) — `board/server/poll.go:38-39` (and `:51-53`) states a consequence the fix does not deliver, contradicted by the car's own test**

&gt; `:38-39` "…and a connected client's current snapshot **must keep showing fresh, precise elapsed time**."
&gt; `:51-53` "…**per-second wire precision** (dom-writer.js) is worth preserving exactly…"

Measured reality, from the car's own pin `elapsedbucket_test.go:99-101`: at a poll where actual elapsed is **50s**, the served snapshot carries **10**. The rendered counter is refreshed at most once per bucket and can trail actual elapsed by up to ~60s. The *value* is exact; its *recency* is not, and the comment conflates the two — precisely for a reader who later asks "why does the elapsed counter not tick?", who will be led away from the answer this fix created.

**I tested the charitable reading before raising this.** Split into clauses: "the WIRE value stays EXACT, unbucketed" — true; "dom-writer.js:201 renders it verbatim to the second" — true (verified at that exact line). The failing clause is the rationale, phrased as a requirement the decision honors ("must keep showing"), which it does not. The car's **commit message** gets it right — *"The wire value stays exact whenever a snapshot is actually served fresh"* — so the precise formulation existed and was lost in the durable artifact. Commit messages are immutable; the comment is the living document, and Law 1 heads this shop's list of real failures.

**Fix:** one sentence — state that between bucket crossings the prior snapshot stands, so the displayed figure can trail by up to the bucket; note that nothing derives from it (`dom-writer.js:200-202` prints, `render.js:188` passes through) and that the alarm-bearing `state` field is exact.

### Minors

**m1 (Minor) — the §12b amendment and the gating matrix are silent on the half of #29 that was not done, and on what the shipped default does to the word "stale."**
The amendment characterises `stale` as *"the genuine alarm; something should be moving and is not."* With `StalenessMs: 15000` (`board/server/config.go:42`) and a shop default budget of `1800` seconds (`config/harness-defaults.json`), that alarm fires **15 seconds into a healthy 30-minute dispatch** and stays lit until it returns. Behaviour is **identical to base** — not a regression, and I will not book it as one — but it means the alarm now reads "a car is running," in the needs-attention register, while the row beside it reads `dispatched` → in-progress. Issue #29's own remedy (*"Also retune stalenessMs against measured reality"*) is untouched and unrecorded as remaining. Add one line to §12b.

**m2 (Minor) — `poll.go:515` cites `output.go:9-32` for `DispatchEntry.MarshalJSON`, which spans `output.go:9-35`** (`return json.Marshal(m)` at `:34`). Range under-covers by three lines; the start is right.

**m3 (Minor) — `gating-matrix.md:44`, Idle row, "Resets on"** says a new in-flight record "flips to `stale` if old". In an append-only store a newly landed record's `at` is ≈ now, so that branch is effectively unreachable; the reachable path is idle → `fresh` → staleness elapses → `stale`. Hedged, so not false — but imprecise on a contract surface.

## 8. CONSTITUTION CHECK

| Law | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| **1. Truth** | **HONORED at the mechanism — and pointedly so.** The constitution's own example of a falsehood is *"a train as **idle** when it is burning tokens."* `hasInFlightDispatch` (`poll.go:428-436`) makes exactly that state unreachable: any `dispatched`/`overdue` winner forces `stale`. Verified — PROBE C (overdue → `stale`) and the #31 scratch store (in-flight → `register-needs-attention`). And the old rendering *was* the falsehood: `first-light.png` shows a fully-returned yard painted red. | Counterpoint, and it is why this is a REJECT: **M2** and **M3** are Law-1 confident falsehoods on durable information surfaces, and **M1** is one on a citation surface. |
| **3. Actionability / HAVAGLANCE** | HONORED. `idle, quiet 3040s` in nominal is a complete single-glance story — state word + honest age + calm register, verbatim-distinct from `stale, Ns`. `#29`'s compose test pins the distinctness by name. | Friction, recorded as **m1**: with `stalenessMs=15000`, "a stalled car looks stalled" degrades to "a running car looks stalled." Pre-existing; #29's untouched second half is the remedy. Also: `3040s` is a poor glance unit, inherited unchanged from `stale, Ns`. |
| **4. Nothing Silently Lost** | HONORED. `idle` *requires* `ageBucketMs` in the schema (`:51`) and renders it (observed: `3040000` → "quiet 3040s") — the age is disclosed, not suppressed, which is what separates this from muting an alarm. #27 loses nothing on the wire: `bucketDispatchesForComparison` deep-copies (`poll.go:506+`) so the served value stays exact (observed 65 and 25 exact, including on an overdue entry). | The one loss — mid-bucket *recency* — is pinned by a test but mis-described by a comment: **M3**. |
| **6. One Truth** | HONORED. `computeLiveFreshness` remains the single freshness derivation (one call site, `poll.go:308`). The browser validator compiles **THE** schema file (`validate.js:7,18-20`), so `idle` has exactly one owner across the language seam — no hand-rolled mirror. `compose.test.js`'s independently-restated `FRESHNESS_CASES` is the sanctioned test-side cross-check, not a second source. The deep copy exists precisely so the comparison basis cannot corrupt the served truth. | — |

## NEW FINDING

### **R2-M1 (MAJOR) — this fix commit minted a fresh dead citation, of the exact class it was fixing, into the file it was fixing**

`board/server/poll.go:43` — the new M3 paragraph:

&gt; `// WHAT THIS DOES NOT MEAN (measured, elapsedbucket_test.go:99-101): …`

At HEAD `9080a5f`, `elapsedbucket_test.go:99-101` is:

```
 99:		t.Fatalf("second poll: %v", err)
100:	}
101:	if changed2 {
```

— an error-propagation guard and a loop into the *seq* assertion. The measurement it promises the reader lands at **`elapsedbucket_test.go:107-109`**:

```
107:	if got := dispatchElapsed(snap2); got != 10 {
108:		t.Fatalf("an undetected-change poll must serve the PRIOR snapshot untouched (elapsed_seconds=10, snap1's value) - got %v", got)
109:	}
```

**Cause, proven both ways.** At `4f06a4c` those exact three lines *were* at 99-101 (`git show 4f06a4c:board/server/elapsedbucket_test.go` confirms). This same commit added +6 net comment lines to that file — the header rewrite and the `now2` block — shifting the target down by 8. The coordinate was lifted verbatim from my round-1 verdict, where it was correct, and not re-derived after the commit's own edit to that very file.

**Why Major, tested against the charitable readings:**
- *"It's only off by eight; the reader will find it."* The reader is being sent to see a **measurement** and lands on `t.Fatalf("second poll: %v", err)` — plumbing that is deceptively test-shaped. That is the same "deceptively field-shaped, unrelated content" the C2R-1 verdict named at `artifacts/reviews/2026-07-23-board-car2-review-round1.md:101`.
- *"It's a comment in a Go file, low blast radius."* Round-1 M1 was citations in a test file and I ruled it Major; C2R-1 was a `$comment` and a Pester header and was ruled Major.
- *"The car disclosed a ripple, so it was diligent."* It disclosed the ripple it caused in `poll.go` and missed the identical ripple it caused in `elapsedbucket_test.go` in the same commit. Disclosed-but-wrong does not clear review, and this one is not disclosed at all.

**Fix — one line, and use your own remedy.** The durable form is the symbol/description one the car already invented and which I verified survives shifts: cite it as *this file's `snap2` assertion (`dispatchElapsed(snap2)` must read 10, not the raw 50)* with no coordinate. Repointing to `107-109` also closes it but re-arms the same trap. **The general rule worth writing into the comment convention: a citation into a file the same commit edits must be re-derived after the edit, or expressed as a symbol.**

The check is sound in both directions: a line carrying a *trailing* comment is retained whole, so a code change on such a line would surface. Four files are **byte-identical in code**.

A message string is evaluated only to be *reported*; it participates in no comparison. **Zero behaviour changed. The car's claim is TRUE.** `go vet ./...` clean corroborates on the Go side.

Condition (a) is **not** met: Majors went **3 → 1** and total findings **7 → 1**.

Substance over letter, and the rule's purpose is to catch defects being *relocated* rather than *resolved*:

- The class was **structurally** addressed, not patched. The car replaced coordinates with symbol references and I verified that remedy holds under exactly the stress that broke the originals — an 11-line shift inside the same commit.
- The instrument is not failing. Line-number citations are checkable, and the check caught this both rounds, cheaply, at the rung it belongs to. Nothing here needs an owner ruling or a different rung.
- Six of seven findings closed with substance and zero drift. That is a converging series with one application miss, not a swirl.

**Cap set, and the conductor is bound by it: round 3 is the last round on this defect class.** If round 3 leaves, or introduces, any further citation-truth defect, that is the instrument failing at this scale and it escalates to the owner instead of a round 4.

## WHY THIS IS AN ESCALATION AND NOT A ROUND 4

**The instrument, named.** Both the car's audit and mine, in every round, were: *extract `path:line` tokens from the ADDED lines of the diff and resolve them.* That method is **structurally blind** to this entire class, because the defective citation is a line **nobody edited** — only its target moved. Three rounds of an adversary explicitly hunting citation truth found zero of these eleven. That is not carelessness; it is a method that cannot see the defect.

**The cap fires on its own terms.** Round 3 *introduced* nothing — but it *leaves* eleven, and the reason is the method, not the care. My round-2 cap: *"If round 3 leaves, or introduces, any further citation-truth defect, that is the instrument failing at this scale and it escalates to the owner instead of a round 4."*

**A round 4 would not fix this.** Hand-repairing eleven coordinates leaves the *next* line insertion to break the *next* set — this train broke coordinates in three separate commits (`3fd4fa6` broke 4, `4f06a4c` broke 7, `9080a5f` broke 1, which I caught as R2-M1). Hand-maintained line coordinates in a repo that constantly edits its own files are **vigilance-tier**, and the Healing Loop already ranks attention below mechanism. The round-3 comment the car just wrote says so itself: *"no amount of care catches that by eye."* It is right, and that sentence is an argument for a gate, not for another round.

**The prior art exists in-repo and I checked before proposing anything.** `docs/templates/repo-policy-check-patterns.md` §1's gate pattern is already instantiated twice — `scripts/tests/DocPolicy.Tests.ps1` and `scripts/tests/CodeCitationPolicy.Tests.ps1`. A citation-**resolver** gate is that same pattern aimed at `path:line` tokens: parse every token in tracked text files, assert the path exists and the file has that many lines, and (the strong form) assert a recorded anchor string still sits at the cited line. This is a ticket, not a car round, and it is the third instance of a landed pattern rather than an invention.

**My own miss, stated plainly.** I ran the wrong audit in round 1 and again in round 2, and in round 2 I prescribed the narrow scope — *"re-resolve that single citation and re-run nothing else"* — which is why round 3 did not look wider. The car did exactly what I asked, well. The gap is mine.

---

## MERGEABILITY AND RECOMMENDED DISPOSITION (the owner's ruling, not mine)

**Not mergeable from `8075640`.** Eleven citation-truth defects stand, three of them supporting a concurrency-safety invariant.

Three dispositions, with my lean stated and the decision explicitly the owner's:

- **(a) Land now, fix later.** Defensible: the *code* is sound and independently verified (rounds 1–2), all suites are green, and none of the eleven is a false claim about *behaviour* — they are dead pointers to true statements. Risk: eleven lying canaries land on `main`, and the repo's own precedent (C2R-1, M1, R2-M1) rejected merges for strictly fewer.
- **(b) One prose commit fixing all eleven, then land, then file the gate.** **My lean.** The discovery cost is already paid — every coordinate and its correct value is enumerated above, so this is mechanical, and the car has already shown it can do the durable form (symbols, or the `git show` base pin). Then the gate ticket prevents round N+1.
- **(c) Build the gate first and let it generate the fix list.** Most rigorous, and it would independently confirm my eleven; costs a train the view work is waiting on.

**The cap binds the conductor: do not dispatch round 4 on this class without an owner ruling.** If the owner rules (b), that is a *directed* prose commit under a ruling, not another adversarial round on the same class — and I would re-review it as a one-shot delta against the enumerated list.

Two tickets are owed regardless of the disposition, per never-drop:
1. **Citation-resolver gate** (`repo-policy-check-patterns.md` §1 pattern, third instance) — the mechanism that retires this class.
2. **Reviewer-method correction** — the citation audit in the reviewer addendum must resolve *every* citation in *every touched file* **and** every citation *targeting* a touched file, not just those on added lines. This finding is the wreckage the fix should be built from.

So they were **already dead before this train touched anything**. Not caused here, not this train's obligation.
- **Exactly three sites, confirmed by my own grep:** `docs/design/2026-07-22-dispatch-harness-design.md:53`, `docs/templates/design-doc.md:204`, `docs/templates/worked-spec.md:94` (coordinate mid-cell).

## FINDING

### **R4-m1 (MINOR, non-blocking) — the new convention comment overstates its own scar**

`board/server/poll.go:104-106`:

&gt; *"…they did, **three separate times across three later trains**, and the disclaimer did not stop it."*

**"Three separate times" is right. "Across three later trains" is not.** I tested it against git history rather than assuming:

- The `#52 C51R-5` comment landed in `5207778`.
- Between then and this train's base `8b9df53`, exactly **one** other commit touched `poll.go` (`9e5261f`, the #30 train) — and the three coordinates were still **exact at `8b9df53`** (I proved this in round 3), so no earlier train broke them.
- All three drifts happened inside **this one train**: `3fd4fa6` (+67/−14), `9080a5f` (+26/−15), `8075640` (+7/−2) — three commits, one train. (`4f06a4c`'s poll.go edits landed below the cited targets and did not shift them.)

Correct text: *"three separate times across one later train's own three commits."* Note the true story is **more** damning, not less — three breakages inside a single train rather than spread thin across three — so the comment understates the density while overstating the breadth.

**Why Minor and not Major, tested for consistency against my own series:** this is not a coordinate — there is nothing to follow and nothing resolves wrongly. Its load-bearing conclusion (*the disclaimer did not stop it; symbols survive insertions, line numbers do not*) is **true and well-evidenced**. I graded comparable substance-right/detail-imprecise items Minor in round 1 (m2, the `output.go:9-32` range under-coverage; m3, the gating-matrix unreachable-branch phrasing), and grading this Major would be inconsistent with that, not stricter. It is a scar with a slightly wrong number, and CLAUDE.md wants the scar accurate — so it should be corrected on the next natural touch of `poll.go`, riding that commit rather than being batched into a cleanup pass that never comes.

## MAJOR-2 — The HAVAGLANCE failure survives, in the very mechanism the commit claims fixes it, undisclosed

This is the centerpiece. I constructed it rather than reasoned about it.

**Construction:** scratch store = every real `artifacts/` record + one subject `zzzz-lost-probe-sorts-last` holding a `presumed-lost` record. Sorts last by `board/fold/algorithm.go:150` (`sort.Strings(subjects)`), so it lands at the bottom of the grid. `presumed-lost` is `needs-attention` in the liveness vocab (`schema/vocab/board-defs.json`). Real Go server, real Chromium, 1920x1080.

**Measured (computed style, real browser):**

```
totalRows: 90        rowsGeometricallyVisible: 35
gridBox: top 97, bottom 273, height 176      gridScrollHeight: 440
hotRowCount: 1
  text:  "zzzz-lost-probe-sorts-lastpresumed-lost"
  color: rgb(255, 74, 58)          &lt;- needs-attention red
  rect:  top 515, bottom 537       &lt;- 242px BELOW the grid's clip edge
  insideGrid: false     VISIBLE_WITHOUT_SCROLLING: false

ALL_LANES:
  lane-dispatches  register-nominal  borderLeft rgb(217,213,201)  title rgb(217,213,201)  "Live"
  lane-gates       register-nominal  borderLeft rgb(217,213,201)  title rgb(217,213,201)  "Live"
  lane-trains      register-nominal  borderLeft rgb(217,213,201)  title rgb(217,213,201)  "Live"
  lane-freight     register-nominal  ...  "Dark"
  lane-fuel        register-nominal  ...  "Bagged"
footerConditions: null
```

**A yard holding a `presumed-lost` dispatch renders a board with not one hot pixel.** I have the screenshot: every lane plate paper-white, every left border paper, all three live lanes reading `idle, quiet 1870s`. The board says *quiet*. From across a room an operator sees a calm yard.

**Answering the brief's sub-question directly:** the lane register does **not** go hot. `render.js:137` composes the lane register from position × freshness × capability only (`compose.js:73-79`); row-level `stateRegister` (`render.js:214`) never enters `mostSevereRegister`. So most-severe-wins does not save the glance here — nothing saves it.

**Counterfactual (runtime style override, no file edited) — the clip is the cause:**

```
.solari-rows { max-height: none !important; overflow-y: visible !important; }
-&gt; hotRow top 515, bottom 537, color rgb(255,74,58), hotRowInViewport: TRUE
-&gt; footerBottom 1068, WHOLE_BOARD_FITS_1080: TRUE
```

At the store size the car itself measured, `max-height: 11rem` is what converts a visible alarm into an invisible one — **and removing it costs nothing**, the whole board still fits in 1080px with 12px to spare.

**Why this is Major and not a NOTE.** Three reasons, and I weighed the counterweight first:

- *The counterweight, stated honestly:* the lane-register semantics are **pre-existing** (`compose.js` untouched — verified, not in diff), and fixing them would be a register-semantics change that #62's scope explicitly forbids. Pre-#62 the row was also off-screen. The car was right not to fix it.
- But the commit **asserts it fixed the HAVAGLANCE failure** ("the exact 'doesn't help much' HAVAGLANCE failure this ticket exists to fix"), and `docs/design/2026-07-23-ui-mockup-brief.md:40-41` sets the acceptance test: *"glanced at from across a room, not studied: one pass must tell an operator whether anything needs them."* Measured, it does not.
- The justification written into the repo is **materially inverted**. `board/web/css/board.css:400-404`:

&gt; `EVERY row is still in the DOM and still reachable (Law 1: "visible, never hidden", same posture as the #30 collapsed-but-present conditions strip)`

  The #30 collapse is Law-1-safe **precisely because severity escapes it**: `dom-writer.js:121` renders `&lt;summary class="board-conditions-summary register-needs-attention"&gt;` carrying `"1 FLAG + 2 notes"` (`render.js:104-108`). The `.solari-rows` scroll clip has **no roll-up whatsoever** — measured `footerConditions: null`. The one property that licenses #30's pattern is the one property the new bound lacks. Left standing, the next car reads this as settled doctrine: *"bounding a lane with `overflow` is Law-1-safe because #30 does it."* That is a Chesterton's fence planted backwards.

**What would have cleared this:** an honest stop / disclosed limitation routed to #1 — exactly what the car did for two *lesser* gaps (store-counts, lane order). It had the mechanism in hand and did not apply it to the largest hole its own change creates. Undisclosed-and-load-bearing.

`board/web/css/board.css:399` and `board/web/css/board.css:427` both cite:

```
board/web/test/screenshots-62/calm-real-store.png

## THE JUDGMENT HALF — my HAVAGLANCE ruling, in plain words

**The looks half: genuinely better, and not templated.** The shared language landed. Wordmark reads at distance; the numbered plates with purpose subtitles are the single best thing here — a stranger now learns what "freight" *means* without asking; the near-black ground is properly dark-room; the footer strip gathers the accounting line where it belongs. Compared to the owner's *"horrible looking"*, this is a real answer.

**The help half: better for the calm case, and I cannot certify it for the alarm case — which is the only case that matters.**

The improvement is real and I measured it: five lanes on one screen instead of four pushed 1,100px below the fold. On a calm yard, one pass genuinely tells you the yard is calm. That is more than the board did yesterday.

But HAVAGLANCE is not "can I see the layout." It is *"can I have a glance and understand everything it is trying to tell me."* On a yard holding a `presumed-lost` dispatch, this board's glance says **quiet**. It is not that the alarm is hard to find in under a second — **there is nothing to find.** Zero hot pixels, five calm plates, three lanes reading *"idle, quiet."* The one-hot-element-findable test cannot be scored because no hot element renders.

And the specific mechanism is the irony: the bounded grid that fixed the crowding is what clips the alarm, on a screen with 280px of unused black below the footer, at a store size where the bound buys nothing. The car traded a board that hid four lanes for a board that hides the alarm.

**None of that makes the bound wrong** — at 300 records it will be necessary, and the real fix (severity roll-up into the lane plate, or hot-rows-first ordering) is register semantics and correctly outside this ticket. What was owed was **disclosure**: name the hole, route it to #1, and do not write a Law-1 justification into the CSS claiming the #30 precedent covers it when the #30 precedent's safety property is exactly what is missing. The car disclosed two smaller gaps and not this one.

Fix MAJOR-1 (one line), MAJOR-3 (repoint two paths), and MAJOR-2's *disclosure* (correct the `board.css` comment, route the hole to #1 with the measurement above), and this is an APPROVE. The visual work is sound and the premise was true.

Three observations a later party cannot re-derive byte-identically, with recipes:

Probe scripts retained at `C:\Users\Chris\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-Chris-git-starcar\15c84ef1-8e84-4d98-aacd-17cce34752cb\scratchpad\{havaglance-attack,counterfactual,premise}.mjs` (scratchpad, not the repo — session-scoped).
2. **Time-dependent values** in my screenshots (`idle, quiet 1870s`, `80 in yard inventory`) drift with wall clock and store growth. The register hexes and the calm/hot lane-class sets do not.
3. **Font resolution is box-specific.** `Bahnschrift` resolved here (Windows). On a box without it the display face degrades to `Arial Narrow`/`sans-serif` — by design, and the car documented it, but my `brandFontFamily` reading is this box's.

Nothing in MAJOR-1, MAJOR-2, or MAJOR-3 depends on any of the above: MAJOR-1 is two `git diff --diff-filter=A` commands, MAJOR-2's核 is `insideGrid:false` + five `register-nominal` classes reproducible from the recipe, MAJOR-3 is `ls`.
